# Mac local processes (owner machine)

**This is the master list.**  Every always-on job, scheduled job, login item,
and installed helper script that agents create on Jay's Mac lives here.
Not Coolify.  Not GitHub-hosted runners.  Not a one-shot `npm exec` from an
interactive session.

- Live: `~/apps/MAC-LOCAL-PROCESSES.md`
- GitHub: https://github.com/Simple-With-Us/AI-Fleet-Coordinator/blob/main/docs/MAC-LOCAL-PROCESSES.md
- Apple Note: `⭐️ Background Jobs Master List` (Coding, pinned; owner-renamed 2026-08-16)
- Binding rule: `~/apps/FLEET-PROTOCOL.md` § Mac local processes


**How to read the Kind column**

- **Always-on** — supposed to stay up across logout/reboot (KeepAlive or a
  login resurrect).  If it is down, that is a bug unless the row says Disabled.
- **Scheduled** — launchd/cron fires it; it is not a long-lived daemon.
- **On-demand** — installed helper.  Agents run it by hand.  It must not linger.

---

## Always-on (supposed to stay up)

Live-checked Fri, Aug 21, 2026 ~2:25am CT.

| Name | Kind | What it is | Live now |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BotFleet Control.app` (`app.botfleet.control.macos`) | **Always-on** (menu-bar controller;  the owner may quit it, and the user-enabled login item brings it back at login) | BF-2520 native AppKit menu bar controller, installed only at `~/Applications/BotFleet Control.app`.  Controls the existing BotFleet Server and its shared/per-bot Local VMs; it never stops the machine-wide Docker or OrbStack runtime.  Opening or quitting the controller does not start or stop the server.  The user may enable its macOS Login Item or hide its menu bar icon.  Source: BotFleet `mac-control/`; build/install helper: `pnpm install:control` (`electron/build-control-app.mjs`).  Installed startup wrapper now supports `BF_CONTROL_STARTUP_POLICY_V1`, after 33 isolated fixture cases passed.  Manages the BotFleet Server, its shared and per-bot Local VMs, and a **Fleet Services** section that watches every long-lived process BotFleet starts on this Mac: the BotFleet MCP server on `127.0.0.1:8794`, managed through pm2 and never by spawning a second copy, the BotFleet Server and its Companion as launchd agents, and the ten pm2 applications the ecosystem file defines.  Each row offers only the actions its observed state allows, a stop the owner asked for is remembered so the reconciler does not take it back, and the BotFleet Server is stopped by asking the server itself rather than by booting its LaunchAgent out.  The server LaunchAgent is loaded, but the harness is deliberately stopped:  `~/.botfleet/stopped.marker` (Oct 25, 2026 5:02pm) holds it down, and the Fleet Services section shows the BotFleet Server as **stopped**. | **Installed and running** from `~/Applications/BotFleet Control.app` — version **1.0.2260** (build 2260), universal arm64+x86_64, Developer ID signed (TeamIdentifier CC8UTF7ATG; `spctl -a -vvv -t exec` **accepted**).  Rebuilt and reinstalled Oct 28, 2026 (MM) from merged `main` at `e68d2623` (BotFleet PR #1387, the Fleet Services work); the Mac suite is 183 tests with 0 failures and the build machine runs the same suite.  The bundle carries **no** trace of the old internal name: 0 files and 0 strings in the binary, where the Oct 21 bundle carried 8.  The menu now shows a **Fleet Services (12)** submenu — BotFleet MCP Server, BotFleet Server, BotFleet Companion, and the ten pm2 applications — with Start, Stop and Restart per service.  The earlier 1.0.2149 bundle was **not** an ancestor of `origin/main`, so its `/api/system/control` call 404'd, Start Local VM stayed greyed out, and the server could not be started from the menu.  The Control app is now also the **fleet services** view: it lists the BotFleet MCP server and keeps it wanted exactly while the BotFleet Server is running (derived from the server's own state, never stored).  **Note:** `electron/build-control-app.mjs` does not auto-install a refreshed Control app — after a merge, rebuild, sign and install it explicitly.  Two things bite in a remote session and both are now known: the installer **deliberately leaves a running copy of the app in place**, so quit it first or the old bundle survives the install; and the version number is the **commit count of the checkout**, so the checkout has to carry the whole history or the version comes out as 1.0.2.  Signing needs the Developer ID, and a remote session cannot reach the login keychain (`errSecInternalComponent`, and `security` reports user interaction is not allowed), so the certificate is fetched from the fleet secrets and imported into a throwaway keychain the way the release workflow does it.  The Mac's pm2 state that the Fleet Services section reads: the MCP server is **stopped** on purpose since Oct 25, 2026, with `~/.botfleet/stopped.marker` present, and the other nine registry applications are online.  |
| `com.jay.claude-remote-control` | Always-on | Phone / claude.ai steering.  Monet / Renoir / Claude all look like `claude`.  Do not SIGKILL because you see no TTY.  `WorkingDirectory` in `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.jay.claude-remote-control.plist` is the default directory new remote/mobile chats start in — changed 2026-09-01 from `~/Code/Socratic.Trade` to `~/Code` (bare `/Users/jay` throws "Workspace not trusted"; `~/Code` is trust-accepted).  Edit that key + `launchctl bootout gui/501/com.jay.claude-remote-control` + `launchctl bootstrap gui/501 <plist>` to repoint it.  **2026-09-27 3:23pm CT (MM) — no longer runs the binary directly; `ProgramArguments` is now `~/apps/claude-remote-control-capped.sh`.**  Reason: Claude Code's OAuth access token lives exactly 8h and `remote-control` loads it into memory once at process start and never re-reads the keychain, so a `KeepAlive` process with no max-runtime presented a dead token every ~8h → 401 → "use /login" → exit → launchd restart.  Four real occurrences logged; the last at 02:51:50 CT 2026-09-27.  A normal `claude` CLI session refreshes the token silently in the same window, which is why it looked arbitrary.  The wrapper SIGTERMs the child at `CLAUDE_REMOTE_CONTROL_MAX_UPTIME` (plist `EnvironmentVariables`, default 21600 = 6h, deliberately under 8h) so KeepAlive restarts it with a fresh keychain read.  Wrapper log: `~/.claude/remote-control.capped.log`.  **Keep the cap under 8h — the whole point is the process must never outlive the token it loaded.**  Paired with the scheduled `com.jay.claude-update-bounce` below, which restarts this job when the binary changes.  **Wed, Sep 30, 2026 4:50pm CT (Claude) — fast-failure backoff.**  From 13:25 CT the CLI reported `loggedIn: false` and every start died in 2–5s with "You must be logged in to use Remote Control"; `KeepAlive` + `ThrottleInterval=10` relaunched the full `claude` binary about 6 times a minute (948 starts in one afternoon) on a Mac already deep in swap.  The wrapper now holds itself alive for 30s, 60s, 120s … capped at 600s before exiting after any non-zero exit under 60s (streak in `~/.claude/remote-control.failstreak`; any run of 60s+ resets it).  After `claude auth login`, `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.claude-remote-control` skips the remaining wait.  Plist unchanged.  **Sun, Oct 18, 12:35am (Claude) — `WorkingDirectory` changed from `~/Code` to `~/apps/lanes` (owner request).**  New remote/mobile chats now start in the lanes folder and the Environment picker shows this job as `lanes`, not `Code`.  The folder is trust-accepted in `~/.claude.json`.  It is a parent folder, not a lane, so a session there still runs `~/apps/lane new <app> <slug>` before editing.  Reason: this job runs `~/.local/bin/claude` (2.1.302 on Oct 18) while the Claude desktop app's own `lanes` entry ran its bundled copy (2.1.299);  one daemon on the newest binary replaces both.  Pre-change plist backed up at `~/.claude/backups/com.jay.claude-remote-control.plist.pre-lanes-2026-10-18`.  **Sun, Oct 18, 9:40pm (Claude) — runs a fixed-path copy of Claude.**  `EnvironmentVariables.CLAUDE_REMOTE_CONTROL_BIN` is now `~/.local/share/claude/stable/claude`, not `~/.local/bin/claude`.  Reason: a bare binary launched by launchd is its own "responsible process", and macOS keys its privacy grants (Desktop, Documents, Downloads, Network Volumes, Google Drive, "data from other apps") to the executable path, so every release at a new `versions/<x>` path re-prompted the owner for every folder.  Every release keeps the same signing requirement (`com.anthropic.claude-code`, team `Q6L2SF6YDW`), so one Full Disk Access grant on the fixed path carries over updates.  `com.jay.claude-update-bounce` keeps that copy current.  Pre-change plist: `~/.claude/backups/com.jay.claude-remote-control.plist.pre-stable-2026-10-18`.  **Thu, Oct 22, 3:51pm (Claude) — the restart waits until no chat is working.**  Every 6h restart ended every chat this job served, with its subagents and background commands, and the chat showed "The container was restarted" (the 11:32am Oct 22 restart killed a running worker that way).  From `CLAUDE_REMOTE_CONTROL_MAX_UPTIME` on, the wrapper now checks once a minute and restarts only when no chat is working:  no Bash tool shell (`/bin/zsh -c source …/shell-snapshots/…`) under the process, and no transcript under `~/.claude/projects/-Users-jay-apps-lanes` written in the last `CLAUDE_REMOTE_CONTROL_IDLE_MINUTES` (default 10).  At `CLAUDE_REMOTE_CONTROL_HARD_UPTIME` (default 27000 = 7h30m) it restarts regardless.  Uptime is wall-clock time:  the old `sleep 21600` did not count Mac sleep, and one run lasted 8h01m on Oct 21.  The CLI's status-line redraws (about 37 MB a day) now go to `~/.claude/remote-control.out`, replaced at each start, and the wrapper log records the CLI's `Error:` lines when it exits non-zero.  `~/.claude/remote-control.log` had reached 443 MB and was cut to its last 1 MB (`~/.claude/remote-control.log.tail-2026-10-22`).  Current Claude Code renews the Remote Control connection itself (its errors page lists "Remote Control could not renew this computer's connection"), so the cap may no longer be needed;  it stays until a run past 8h is seen to keep working.  Pre-change wrapper:  `~/.claude/backups/claude-remote-control-capped.sh.pre-idle-2026-10-22`.  **Open (owner):**  the Claude desktop app still serves `~/apps/lanes` too (Settings › Claude Code › "Use this computer from your phone and claude.ai"), so the app and this job fight over the folder.  Whichever starts second fails:  the app logs `register failed (folder_taken)` every minute in `~/Library/Logs/Claude/remote-control.log`, and this job exits with "This folder is already served by the Claude app on this device" and backs off, leaving `lanes` offline for up to 10 minutes at a time (10 fast failures on Oct 22, 9 on Oct 18).  While this job is down, the chats it served stop responding until it runs again in the same folder (Remote Control docs, "Resume sessions after stopping the server").  Remove `lanes` from the app's list;  the Oct 18 change meant this job to replace the app's entry. | **Up** (the running process keeps the old wrapper until its next restart) |

Without it a seat cannot be told an Operator-opened direct message from a seat-opened one, and the refusal names the setting instead of the rule. Seat Zulip keys come from Infisical Fleet-OPS `/zulip` `<BOT>_ZULIP_*` through `fleet-secrets`, fetched once at start and held in memory (90-second cap);  `SENTRY_FLEET_DSN` comes from `/agent-sync` (cached for the process life, never logged). A global `wakes_only` pause in `~/.agent-sync/listener/pause.json`, set at 21:16 on Oct 20, 2026, had also been dropping every wake for every seat on this Mac; it is lifted, and `simple-chat daemon resume` is how to lift one again.  The MM seat's budget is 5 cold wakes an hour and 80 a day, because with the gate off the caps are the only thing that can still silently hold a wake.  Those caps count cold wakes only: a message that arrives while the seat already has a live session on that topic is delivered into that session and costs no wake and no budget slot. It exists because the MiniMax Code Desktop app's own Feishu binding never finished: that path waits on a Lark app-registration device code (the runtime calls `POST /oauth/v1/app/registration` on `open.feishu.cn` and hands Jay a `page/cli` code), and when the registration is not completed the Connection stays in state `pending`, no `physicalBindings` entry is written, no `feishu-channel.yaml` is created and no Feishu WebSocket client ever starts, so the bot never answers.  The native binding is also keyed on a `projectKey`, which is why changing the project folder forced Jay to create a new bot; this bridge keeps one bot and one session regardless of folder.  Loop: `lark-cli event consume im.message.receive_v1 --as bot` (Open Platform WebSocket, NDJSON on stdout) -> one MiniMax Code session per Lark chat over the Agent Client Protocol (`mcode acp`, `session/new` + `session/prompt`, `permissionMode` `bypassPermissions` by default) -> `lark-cli im +messages-reply --as bot` back to the same message.  Turns inside one chat are serialized, so a second message waits for the first answer.  In a group chat the bot answers only when it is mentioned; in a one-to-one chat every message is for the bot.  Credentials: none in the plist and none read from `~/.secrets`; the Lark app secret stays inside lark-cli's own encrypted store (`~/Library/Application Support/lark-cli/appsecret_*.enc`, key in the login keychain).  lark-cli 1.0.98 is installed per-user at `~/.local/npm-global/bin/lark-cli` (a global npm install fails EACCES on this Mac because `/usr/local/lib/node_modules` is root-owned; never escalate with sudo).  Allowed sender is pinned to the owner's Lark open_id via `--allow-sender`; the bot's own open_id goes in `--bot-open-id` for mention detection.  State (seen message ids) lives in `~/.minimax/lark-mm-bridge/state.json`.  Logs `~/Library/Logs/lark-mm-bridge/bridge.{err,out}.log`; the Lark event bus keeps its own log at `~/.lark-cli/events/cli_aa5db9fd8f38c08e/bus.log`.  A plist edit needs `launchctl bootout` then `bootstrap` (bootstrap straight after a bootout races and fails with an I/O error).  **Mon, Oct 19, 2026 2:30am (MM, AFC-417):** the bridge was crash-looping on every inbound message — `argparse` derives `allow_sender` from `--allow-sender` while the code read `args.allowed_sender`, so the first event raised `AttributeError` and launchd restarted it straight back into the crash (the "consumer churn" in `bus.log` was this, not an lark-cli fault).  The dest is now named explicitly, and the tests build their namespaces from the bridge's own parser instead of hand-built stand-ins, which is what let the mismatch ship.  A plist edit still needs `launchctl bootout` then `bootstrap` (bootstrap straight after a bootout races and fails with an I/O error).  **Mon, Oct 19, 2026 3:00am (MM, AFC-417):** three more fixes.  (1) The LaunchAgent ran as `ProcessType Background`, which starves a CPU-heavy ACP handshake on a loaded Mac, so `initialize` timed out at 120s and the seat answered "could not answer"; it now runs as `Standard`, the handshake gets 300s, and the ACP server's stderr goes to the bridge log instead of `DEVNULL`.  (2) The bridge answers the requests the ACP server sends to the client, because nobody is watching Lark to click a permission prompt and an unanswered request stalled the turn until the prompt timeout; a permission request is answered with its allow option, since the owner has set full access for this seat.  (3) The reply text carries the owner's sentence gap as two ASCII spaces (Lark renders a plain text message literally, and the owner asked for exactly that on Oct 19, 2026: "No nbsp here please just 2 spaces").    **Mon, Oct 19, 2026 5:55pm (MM, AFC-417):** the bridge now mirrors every Lark exchange into a Zulip topic named for the chat, carrying the MiniMax session id, and the Lark reply ends with that same session id, so the owner can see the Lark side from Zulip and can tell which MiniMax thread answered.  The mirror posts with `simple-chat` and the nbsp gap, while the Lark reply keeps the two ASCII spaces the owner asked for on Oct 20, 2026.  The Lark write timeout went from 120s to 300s and the seat turn from 600s to 1200s, because at a load of 190 lark-cli took 54s and then lost the acknowledgement, which lost the owner's 3:18pm PING.  A failed command now reports its whole stderr instead of its last line, which for lark-cli was the closing brace of a JSON error.  The bridge imports the fleet secret scanner from the managed agent-sync runtime, so it adds that checkout to `sys.path` before the import.  **Thu, Oct 22, 2026 8:36pm CT (MM):** the eyes marker and the Operator's signal path.  A Lark message whose first character is the eyes emoji (U+1F440) is read and dropped:  no turn, no reply, no delivery row, and the text never reaches the secret scan or the log.  The marker counts only at the start of the trimmed text, so one later in the line is an ordinary message.  The Operator (the always-on fleet listener) can now put a message into the Lark chat:  it runs this script with `--signal` and the text, which leaves a JSON file in the `signal/` directory under the state root, and the bridge sends it into the chat with an idempotency key built from the signal's own id, runs the seat turn on it, and puts the answer back in the same chat.  A signal that names no chat goes to the chat the owner last wrote in, and that chat is remembered across restarts.  A credential-shaped signal, an empty one, or one with nowhere to go is refused and filed with its reason.  Also: the live copy still pointed at the old `~/.local/bin/agent-sync`;  the Zulip mirror now uses `~/.local/bin/simple-chat`, the name the hard cutover gave it.  | **Up** (launchd KeepAlive) |

| `com.jay.botfleet-update` | On-demand (one-shot) | **Oct 8, 2026 (CLAUDE), branch `claude/updater-drain` (board `ef645faf`, PR #960):** the desktop **Install Update** button no longer sends `force`, so the job is submitted without `--force` and runs the default hold, 60-second grace, then pause-and-resume; a busy Mac no longer disables the button (`capabilities.busy` only changes the copy), and the progress record gains a `detail` line ("Waiting for 3 bots to finish").  **Added Sun, Sep 13, 2026 (CLAUDE), BotFleet branch `claude/remote-update`.**  Not a plist and not always-on: the BotFleet harness submits it with `launchctl submit -l com.jay.botfleet-update -o <log> -e <log> -- /bin/bash -c 'export PATH=<node dir>:"$PATH"; exec /bin/bash ~/apps/update-botfleet.sh --progress <file> --run-id <id>'` when someone presses **Install Update** in the desktop app or taps it on the paired phone (`POST /api/update/run`).  It runs the same transactional updater `ubf` runs, and it has to be a launchd job rather than a child of anything: the updater stops `com.jay.botfleet-server` and quits `/Applications/BotFleet.app`, so a child of either would die mid-install.  launchd owns it instead, in the same GUI domain.  The job writes step-by-step progress and a final outcome to `~/Library/Caches/BotFleet/update-control/runs/<runId>.progress.json` with its stdout and stderr beside it in `<runId>.log`; the harness reads those back, which is also how a run survives the harness restart it performs.  `current-run.json` / `last-run.json` / `available.json` in that same directory are the harness's own bookkeeping.  The label is removed (`launchctl remove`) before each submit, and a submit failure falls back to a detached `spawn` of the same script.  Nothing loads it at boot and nothing keeps it alive; after a run it stays registered until the next submit removes it. | **Not yet in use** — lands with BotFleet PR; no run has been started from this Mac. |
| BotFleet background jobs (`~/.botfleet/jobs/`, processes carrying `BOTFLEET_JOB_ID`) | On-demand (owned by the harness) | **Added Thu, Oct 1, 2026 (CLAUDE), BotFleet branch `claude/jobs-registry` (jobs P1).**  Not a plist, pm2 job or cron row: the BotFleet harness (`com.jay.botfleet-server`, or the server BotFleet.app forks) starts one when an HTTP-lane bot (MiniMax, Grok API, OpenAI-compatible) with This Computer calls `job_start`.  Each job is a `/bin/sh` wrapper leading its own process group, run as `nice -n 10 taskpolicy -c utility` with a `ulimit -t` CPU cap and only the model shell environment plus `BOTFLEET_JOB_ID=job_<ulid>`.  Output goes to `~/.botfleet/jobs/<id>.log` (0600, checked twice a second while jobs run and cut back past 8 MiB; a job printing faster than 64 MiB a second is stopped), the exit code to `<id>.exit` (written atomically), metadata to `jobs.json` (at most 500 records; a deleted conversation's or bot's records and logs go with it).  Caps: 3 running per thread, 4 per bot, 8 per host; 60-minute default limit (240 max; owner config up to 6 h), counted in awake time.  Admission refuses at swap >= 98% (default; this Mac idles near 90-94%), < 2 GB free disk, or a tripped spend ceiling; there is no load gate.  Lifecycle: the harness stops every job (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 5 s) when it quits or restarts, an update's restart included, and marks it lost (the updater's quiesce alone does not, so a rolled-back update loses nothing); boot settles from exit files and kills a leader only when its start time matches; a group whose leader is gone is never signalled by its number (that number may be someone else's group after a reboot) — the sweep stops exactly the processes carrying that job's `BOTFLEET_JOB_ID`; its own 5-minute sweep stops any stray process still carrying the id of a finished job of that data folder.  **Do not kill these as `ppid=1` leaks**: a job whose harness is alive is the harness's to stop, and one whose harness died is stopped at the next boot or sweep.  Config: `jobs` in `~/.botfleet/config.json` (`enabled`, `wake` kill switch, `defaultMinutes`, `maxMinutes`, `cpuCores`, `admission.maxSwapPercent`, `admission.minFreeDiskMb`).  Janitor: no automated fleet job reaps arbitrary orphans, so nothing needed a skip rule; the manual policy in "Not in this list" names the exception. | On demand; nothing runs until a bot starts a job (live once the PR merges and the harness restarts) |

| `~/.zshenv` (the `GH_TOKEN` refresh) | Always-on | Shell init, not a process.  **Changed Oct 21, 2026 (MM, board AFC-460, closed).**  Every zsh sources this file - interactive shells and the non-interactive `zsh -c` calls agent tools spawn alike - so it sets the `GH_TOKEN` that a seat's `gh` and `git` see, and its per-shell cost is paid on every one of those invocations.  launchd LaunchAgents never source it:  no plist under `scripts/launchd/` sets `GH_TOKEN`, so those jobs keep whatever token the launchd session holds.  Plists and launchers stay value-blind:  a launchd job that needs a token resolves it at runtime - process environment first, then `fleet-secrets get GITHUB_TOKEN` - and passes it only through the child environment, never argv and never a literal value in a plist or any tracked file.  **Approved resolution order (binding).**  A nonempty inherited `GH_TOKEN` wins;  only when it is absent or empty does the shell resolve `GITHUB_TOKEN` from the fleet store,  and the shell fails closed with `GH_TOKEN is unavailable` when neither source resolves.  That is the fleet default (`FLEET-PROTOCOL.md`, Reading Keys Safely,  and `docs/protocols/cli-login-sync.md`),  and this shell is the one documented exception to it,  approved by Jay on Oct 21, 2026:  in practice a nonempty fleet-store value wins whenever the store answers with one,  because the environment token this Mac inherited is the stale GUI-session token that answered `HTTP 401` on every call.  An empty store result or an unreachable store leaves the inherited token alone and never clobbers it.  What this file exports is also what git pushes with,  because git's credential helper hands out the exported variable when it is set;  **The identity** says what that means on Oct 22, 2026.  The GitHub CLI credential store (`~/.config/gh/hosts.yml`, read with `gh auth token`) is not an approved secret source, and a personal PAT imported from it is not machine-identity bootstrap material.  **What is implemented.**  The store key is `GITHUB_PAT` under `/github`;  there is no `GH_TOKEN` key in the store, so the store value is read as `GITHUB_PAT` and exported under both names, because the repo tools read `GITHUB_TOKEN` first (`scripts/kody-deferred-sweep.mjs`, `scripts/kody-defer/defer.mjs`) and the GitHub CLI reads `GH_TOKEN`.  The store's value wins whenever the store answers, because the environment token this Mac inherited is the stale GUI-session token that answered `HTTP 401` on every call, and keeping it is the fault the change exists to fix.  The environment token is preserved when the store cannot be reached, and an empty result never clobbers it.  **The cache.**  The store read costs about 1.6 seconds, so it sits behind a 300 second TTL cache at `~/.cache/fleet/gh-token`,  written with `umask 077` at mode 600.  It holds one value,  the store's,  copied verbatim,  and this shell is its only reader;  it is the short-TTL cache the Oct 21 ruling calls for,  it expires on its own,  and it is not a credential source for anything else.  The fleet's standing rule keeps a secret out of a flat file,  and this cache is the one documented departure from it,  approved with the order on Oct 21, 2026.  The store is read at most once per interval, not once per shell.  **Verified.**  A fresh shell resolves a token that answers `HTTP 200` and `gh api user` returns `jaywedgeworth22`;  a second shell starts in 18 milliseconds off the cache;  with the store unreachable and no inherited token the shell fails closed with `GH_TOKEN is unavailable` and exports nothing;  with the store unreachable and an inherited token the token is preserved and mirrored into `GITHUB_TOKEN`.  **Why it changed.**  `gh auth token` returns the environment's own token when one is set, so the old `[ -z "${GH_TOKEN:-}" ]` guard kept the stale GUI-session token that answered `HTTP 401: Bad credentials` on every call, blocking `gh` and `git push` for the interactive seats on this Mac.  **No credential changed.**  None was minted, moved or re-synced.  The resolved value is cached on disk at mode 600 under `~/.cache/fleet/` for the 300 second interval, which is the short-TTL cache the ruling calls for;  no secret value appears in this row.  **The identity.**  The fleet store's `GITHUB_TOKEN` is a fine-grained personal access token for `jaywedgeworth22`:  93 characters,  the `github_pat_` shape,  no scope header,  and `gh api user` returns `jaywedgeworth22`.  It authenticates and it reads,  and it writes nothing:  a push with it is refused with 403 (`Permission to Simple-With-Us/AI-Fleet-Coordinator.git denied to jaywedgeworth22`,  verified on Oct 22, 2026 with a dry-run push against this repository),  an API write with it is refused the same way (`Resource not accessible by personal access token`),  and `GITHUB_ADMIN_PAT` and `GITHUB_RUNNER_PAT` are refused the same way.  The store's `GITHUB_PAT` under `/github` is the universal personal access token Jay added on Oct 22,  2026 for the GitHub work:  the classic shape,  40 characters,  and a push with it was verified against this repository on Oct 22.  That is the value this shell resolves and exports,  so a shell that sources `~/.zshenv` pushes with it.  The credential that pushes when this file has exported nothing is the classic personal access token in the GitHub CLI's own credential store (`gh auth token`),  and git's helper hands that one to git only when this file has not exported a variable:  git's helper hands that one to git only when this file has not exported a variable,  and the same push with `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` unset is accepted.  `FLEET-PROTOCOL.md` (Pushing Workflow Files) tells an agent to fetch `GITHUB_TOKEN` from the store for a workflow push,  and that value cannot push;  the rule lives in the Fleet-OPS handbook,  so this row records the fact and the correction lands there.  The token this Mac inherited from the GUI session was the stale one that answered 401 on every call,  and that is the fault this change exists to fix.  **Revert.**  `cp ~/.zshenv.bak-before-gh-token-20261021 ~/.zshenv`.  The durable piece is Jay's and it is not this row's.  The mechanism of record is the per-seat CLI login sync  (`docs/protocols/cli-login-sync.md` and `scripts/fleet_secrets/sync_cli_login.py`),  which keeps a seat's own `GH_TOKEN` under `/cli-logins/<SEAT>` and hands it to the environment on every host that seat works on.  A seat's own login is written from `--from file` (a 0600 file the owner hands over) or from the seat's own store path;  `--from env`,  which is the script's default,  would copy this shell's exported token,  and that is the single fleet-store value,  so it is not a per-seat source.  `git-relay` is the consumer of those per-seat logins,  it is nearly done,  and it will mint them in the background,  but it is not in the protocol yet,  so this row does not claim it as the mechanism.  A seat does handle its own credential:  the sync is how,  and the shape of that credential is git-relay's business,  not this row's.  AFC-460 is closed on the outage;  the store resolution in this row is what carries the shell until the per-seat sync is.  The mechanism and the evidence are on fleet recall as `contrib/MM/2026-10-21/0000404e`,  and the git-relay ruling is `contrib/MM/2026-10-22/38025aac`. | Installed Oct 21, 2026 (shell init; every zsh sources it) |

| `com.jay.disk-janitor` | **Retired 2026-10-10** (was every 30 min) | **Retired 2026-10-10; HogHunter takes over and manages these scripts directly** (owner Sun, Oct 11).  No plist, not loaded;  taken out of the `mac-process-watch.sh` expect lists Sun, Oct 11, 2026 (CLAUDE).  History below is kept for reference.  Regenerable-cache + CleanMyMac CLI + Hog Hunter reclaim-engine sweep + idle-worktree cleanup when disk is tight (< 80G free; pressure at 65G).  **2026-10-04 10:55pm CT (MINIMAX, board `e30a863e`, AFC #331): the CleanMyMac CLI is back.**  It is a **free public beta** (MacPaw, v1.0.0, released 2026-07) and Jay asked for it once he learned that; reinstalled with `brew reinstall --cask cleanmymac-cli` (v1.0.0, `CleanMyMac_5_CLI.app` → `/opt/homebrew/bin/cleanmymac` and `cmm`).  It had been silently dead for weeks, and the reason is worth keeping: `brew list` showed a `cleanmymac-cli` row, so the janitor recorded the CLI as "uninstalled" only because `command -v cleanmymac` exited 1 — the cask receipt was **0 bytes**, the app bundle was gone but brew kept the row, and every run logged as if it had cleaned something while doing nothing.  The `dev`/`ai`/`trash` sweep now runs again **alongside** the Hog Hunter engine, not instead of it: Hog Hunter owns in-place log truncation, APFS snapshot pruning and the graded band, while the CLI walks 21 dev cache trees (Homebrew, npm, Yarn, pnpm, pip, Cargo, Go, CocoaPods, Docker, VS Code, JetBrains, Maven, Gradle, Poetry, uv, Bun, Deno, mise) plus AI-tool junk.  Each call is bounded at 120s via `janitor_watchdog`.  **Two bans deliberately stand:** `junk` is never swept (the 2026-09-13 bug was `junk` unlinking a log launchd still held open, so the daemon wrote to an unlinked inode and freed nothing — the CLI has no in-place truncation rule, so the Hog Hunter `logs` rule is the only safe path for logs), and **`optimize ram` is never called, structurally and not behind a gate** — it purges resident pages into swapfiles on the same APFS container as user data, and it was measured during this change with swap already at 94% (13.4 of 14 GB) and the data volume at 97% full, so it converts a RAM problem into a disk problem.  `--force` is required and is **not** a shortcut: the review step is not a safety gate without a TTY — with stdin closed the CLI scans, renders its review screen, reads EOF and deletes anyway (verified here, 3 items / 37 KB), so `--force` is what makes the behaviour explicit instead of accidentally interactive.  New `scripts/test-disk-janitor-cleanmymac-safety.sh` in AFC holds those rules (no `junk`, no `optimize ram`, every call watchdog-bounded, only dev/ai/trash, guard still `if command -v cleanmymac` so a missing binary is loud next time).  Not yet exercised on a real low-free run: this Mac was at load ~350 and 97% disk during the change, so the `JANITOR_MAX_LOAD=250` pressure gate correctly suppresses it until the Mac calms down.  Walks fleet Code repos including BotFleet/fleet-ops.  Kimi-named / nested `.claude/worktrees` / `/tmp` scratch reaped when clean.  `STALE_DAYS=2` under 80G free.  Steal a run-lock older than 2h.  Sat, Sep 12, 2026 1:20pm CT (Claude): `KEEP_RE` now also protects `~/apps/botfleet-server` (the always-on harness checkout is clean and idle by construction, so the pressure reap deleted its dependencies twice this week).  Sat, Sep 12, 2026 4:50pm CT (Claude): the low-free CleanMyMac step no longer runs bare `cleanmymac clean --force` (which ran its `junk` module and deleted the live, launchd-held-open `~/Library/Logs/botfleet/server.log` out from under `com.jay.botfleet-server` on 2026-09-12 ~12:31) — it now runs only `cleanmymac clean dev --force`, `cleanmymac clean ai --force`, and `cleanmymac clean trash --force` (never system junk), plus `cleanmymac optimize ram` as before.  Also added `~/Library/Logs`, `~/Library/Logs/botfleet`, and `~/.botfleet` to CleanMyMac's own ignore list (`cleanmymac ignore add`, now 20 entries) as a second layer against any other `clean junk` caller — including `~/apps/mac-auto-cleanup.sh`'s own unconditional `cleanmymac clean --force`, which still runs it and is flagged separately, unfixed here.   **Wed, Sep 30, 2026 (Claude), AFC #311:** pressure gate.  Right after the lock, if load1 > `JANITOR_MAX_LOAD` (40) or swap ≥ `JANITOR_MAX_SWAP_PCT` (90%), it logs `PRESSURE-SKIP`, runs only `janitor_cheap_truncations` (pm2-log cap + vitest temp-DB reap), and exits 0 — no git/gh/find-over-worktrees, no `du`, no cache sweep, no CleanMyMac.  Stuck-holder steal and `janitor_watchdog` now kill the whole process tree (via `pgrep -P`), and `optimize ram` needs `RESOURCE_ALLOW_RAM_OPTIMIZE=1`.  Live copy re-synced from main by atomic `mv`; the unlanded 2026-09-25 BF-HOUSEKEEPER edits were merged into the PR.  Backup `~/.claude-disk-janitor/janitor.sh.bak-20260930`.  **Fri, Oct 2, 2026 6:05am CT (MINIMAX), board ce1b42b1:** the Sep 30 pressure gate was tripping on the wrong arm.  It is an OR (`load1 > JANITOR_MAX_LOAD` **or** `swap >= JANITOR_MAX_SWAP_PCT`), and `JANITOR_MAX_LOAD` defaulted to 40, which is below what this Mac sustains by design since it runs 40+ concurrent agent sessions.  Every 30-min tick logged `PRESSURE-SKIP` and retired nothing, including the run at `2026-10-01 22:10` which skipped at **load=10.81** purely because swap was 93%.  Added an `EnvironmentVariables` block to `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.jay.disk-janitor.plist` setting `JANITOR_MAX_LOAD=250`; swap remains the hard gate at its 90% default.  Plist backed up to `~/apps/_archive/2026-10-02/com.jay.disk-janitor.plist.bak-2026-10-02`, validated with `plutil -lint`, reloaded with `launchctl bootout`/`bootstrap`.  `janitor.sh` itself was not changed.  Still-on: scheduled, every 30 min.  Note for the next mirror sync: the tracked copy in `docs/MAC-LOCAL-PROCESSES.md` still carries the old text.  **Outcome, Sat, Oct 2, 2026 8:00pm CT (MINIMAX):** the change works.  Before it, every tick for days took PRESSURE-SKIP and retired nothing.  After it, 8 of 37 runs on Oct 2 executed the full path, and the 19:52 run went `wtprune` -> `wt-retire` (`n_reap=0`, nothing was eligible) -> `lowfree` -> `pressure` -> `done`, reclaiming 5G and taking free disk 32G -> 38G.  The load arm is genuinely fixed; swap is now the only arm that can trip it, which is the intended design, since the gate exists to back off under real memory pressure.  Worth knowing it now reaches `lowfree`, which calls `~/Code/HogHunter/scripts/hoghunter-clean` (CleanMyMac itself is uninstalled); that path is cache-only and has never taken a window.  **Same day, 8:20pm CT (MINIMAX) — the gate is now GRADED, not binary, on the owner's call to favour trying to clean.**  The catch-22: refusing to clean is exactly what let this Mac get too loaded to clean, and both old thresholds (load 40, swap 90) sat AT its normal operating level, so 29 of 37 ticks on Oct 2 skipped and 55 of 88 logged swap between 90% and 94%.  The pile-up the janitor exists to prevent was being caused by the janitor standing down.  Three arms now: **hard** (load1 > 250 or swap >= 98) cheap truncations only, then exit; **lean** (load1 >= 150 or swap >= 90) skip only the five read-only `du` probes but STILL prune and retire old merged worktrees and still run the disk-pressure cache sweeps; **full** otherwise, unchanged.  Retirement is the phase worth running under load because it is watchdog-bounded (30s per fetch, 30s per remove), demands a clean tree plus `STALE_DAYS` idle plus a genuinely merged head, and cannot lose a branch or a commit — while the `du` probes free nothing themselves, so dropping them costs no reclaim.  Verified in the real environment, not simulated: a launchd-driven run at `load=59.90 swap=94%` logged `LEAN-MODE` and proceeded through `wtprune` and `wt-retire` where the old gate would have logged `PRESSURE-SKIP` and exited.  Live script backed up to `~/apps/_archive/2026-10-02/janitor.sh.bak-minimax-2026-10-02`; merged as AFC PR #318, which also re-synced the tracked copy, which had drifted 164 lines and was missing the 2026-09-30 `janitor_kill_tree` process-tree fix and the `AI-Fleet-Coordinator` case fix in `KEEP_RE`.  **Sat, Oct 10, 2026 8:49am (CLAUDE, board `1813a9d0`, AFC #449):**  lanes stored on the external disk (`~/apps/lanes/<Repo>` as a symlink onto `/Volumes/External/Lanes/<Repo>`, setting `FLEET_LANES_EXTERNAL_ROOT`) count as nested lanes, so git's real-path spelling of such a lane stays doctor-gated and never retires on the legacy tests.  `git worktree prune` is now skipped, with a `PRUNE-SKIPPED` line in the log, while a link onto the disk leads nowhere or the disk is not mounted, because prune deletes the registry entry of every worktree whose folder is missing.  The doctor run gets the setting too.  **Sat, Oct 10, 2026 9:05am (CLAUDE, AFC #450):**  the prune skip also covers a relative dangling link and a lane that kept its old registered `~/apps/lanes/<Repo>/<lane>` path (a prune guard that only looked for the external root missed both);  external lane links must be absolute.  Live copy re-synced from main (2367b5c6;  backup `~/apps/_archive/2026-10-10/janitor.sh.bak-extlanes-prune`). |

| `com.jay.reaper-watch` | always-on (launchd, KeepAlive) | **Added Fri, Oct 2, 2026 8:02pm CT (MINIMAX), board 912034a0 follow-up.**  Headless watcher that names the PID responsible when a directory disappears from `~/apps`, because the responsible process logs nothing: the disk-janitor, code-main-keeper and mac-auto-cleanup were all ruled out, and no Claude / Codex / Grok / Gemini / Cursor transcript contains a removal command for the lane that vanished.  One `ps` per second, retaining only processes whose command line matches a removal pattern in a 12 s rolling window, so a short-lived remover is still attributable after it has exited; on a hit it logs the victim plus every suspect and its full ppid ancestry back to pid 1.  **Verified, not assumed:** `test-attr.py` asserts the PID is actually captured and passes on a 12,000-file, 2.1 s removal (`PASS: attributed the deletion to pid 547`); an earlier build that reported "no process seen" on small probes was indistinguishable from a broken watcher, which is exactly why the assertion is there.  Runs as the user, no root, no Endpoint Security, no GUI, so it never surfaces a window on the owner's screen.  The kernel-level alternatives (`fs_usage`, `eslogger`) both need root and SIP off, which is why this polls instead.  Log `~/apps/_archive/2026-10-02/reaper-watch.log` (rotates at 12 MiB), script `reaper-watch.py` beside it, pidfile `reaper-watch.pid`.  **Disable:** `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.reaper-watch`.  **Do not stop it** while board 912034a0 stays open; it is the only instrument on this Mac able to attribute the deletion. | **On; up (pid 12958)** |

| `com.jay.claude-update-bounce` | every 5 min | Restarts `com.jay.claude-remote-control` when the Claude Code binary changes underneath it.  `~/apps/claude-update-bounce.py` (`/opt/homebrew/bin/python3`, `ProcessType: Background`, `RunAtLoad: false`).  Compares `os.path.realpath("~/.local/bin/claude")` against `~/.claude/.remote-control-bin-version`; on change writes the new value then `launchctl kickstart -k gui/501/com.jay.claude-remote-control`.  **Deliberately updater-agnostic** — it does not care who updated Claude (native updater, `com.github.domt4.homebrew-autoupdate`, mise, a manual `claude update`), only that the resolved binary path moved, so it stays correct for updaters that do not exist yet.  Added 2026-09-27 (MM) because a native update (2.1.282 → 2.1.283, `path: native`) landed 13:17 CT under a process that had been resident 11h, on a now-deleted 2.1.282 inode — and `~/.claude.json` has `autoUpdates: false`, so Claude Code's own updater is not the actor.  First run only records a baseline and does not bounce.  Log: `~/.claude/update-bounce.log`.  On-demand dry run: `~/apps/claude-update-bounce.py --check`.  **Sun, Oct 18, 9:40pm (Claude) — also keeps the fixed-path copy.**  Each run, before the bounce check, `sync_stable()` copies the resolved binary to `~/.local/share/claude/stable/claude` when it is missing or stale (marker `~/.claude/.stable-claude-version`), verifies Anthropic's signature with `codesign --verify -R`, and swaps it in with an atomic rename so a running process keeps its old inode.  A failed copy or signature check is logged and leaves the old copy in place.  Pre-change script: `~/.claude/backups/claude-update-bounce.py.pre-stable-2026-10-18`.
| `com.jay.mac-process-watch` | every 120 s | Always-on restarter + scheduled-timer keeper.  pm2 via `~/.pm2/pm2.pid` + `kill -0` (do not pgrep -f).  launchd always-on: bootstrap / kickstart, except the BotFleet harness `app.botfleet.server` (legacy label `com.jay.botfleet-server` accepted as an alias), which is UP when `127.0.0.1:8799/health` is 200 (do not steal the port from BotFleet.app).  The harness is skipped while `~/Library/Caches/BotFleet/update.lock` is held by a live pid, so the watch never re-bootstraps it under a running BotFleet update (a lock older than 2 h is ignored as stale), and a bootstrap that answers already-loaded (EIO 5) counts as healthy, not as a failure.  AFC #314 (`fac5448`), installed Fri, Oct 2, 2026 ~12:05am CT; the stale copy had been re-bootstrapping the harness from the legacy plist mid-update.  Scheduled: bootstrap if not-loaded; **idle is OK**.  Never bootstrap `com.jay.ios-ship-now` or `com.PM2`.  Steals janitor/shepherd locks >2h.  Checks trigger script paths exist.  Backoff 4/hour.  `MAC_PROCESS_WATCH_RESTART=0` = log-only.  **HTTP health is slow-vs-dead (2026-10-08, CLAUDE):** the local `/health` probe for `mac-collab` (:8792) and `xcode-health` (:8791) restarts the pm2 job at once only when curl is refused (rc 7).  A timeout or any other error logs `SLOW  pm2:<name>  curl=<rc> streak=<n>/<K>` and needs K consecutive strikes before the restart (default 3, override `MAC_PROCESS_WATCH_HTTP_SLOW_STRIKES`; streaks in `~/Library/Logs/mac-process-watch.state.http-slow`).  Why: under load one timed-out probe ran `pm2 restart`, the starved server could not exit, pm2 SIGKILLed it, and the 2-4 minute cold start caused the real connection-refused outages.  Slack-era entries (`agent-sync-push`, `cursor-slack-sync`, `com.jay.slack-agent-inbox` and its two trigger scripts) and the retired `homebrew.mxcl.moshi-hook` are no longer watched, and a stale `~/.dsh/profiles/node_modules.lock` (>10 min) is stolen.  **Shellular relay check is confirm-then-kill (2026-10-08, CLAUDE; AFC #380, installed live Fri, Oct 9, 12:10am):** the pid is killed only when relay-failure lines (error log: handshake / `Relay wss://... failed` / `No relay responded`; out log: `Retrying in`) are strictly newer than the latest `Connected` / `Reconnected` / client-connected line, the latest `Shellular CLI v` start banner and the process start time, the evidence is under 20 minutes old, and it stands on two watch passes at least 120s apart with no recovery between them (streak file `~/Library/Logs/mac-process-watch.state.shellular`).  180s of grace follows any (re)start and the 4-per-hour backoff still applies.  A fresh `Connection lost ... Reconnecting` alone never counts.  Why: the old rule killed the pid mid-reconnect 17 times in one day (29 `DOWN  pm2:shellular  status=relay-dead` lines) although Shellular normally reconnects within seconds.  Logs: `SLOW  pm2:shellular  relay-suspect` on the first pass, `DOWN ... relay-dead` only when confirmed.  Knobs: `MAC_PROCESS_WATCH_SHELLULAR_CONFIRM_SEC` (120), `_GRACE_SEC` (180), `_STALE_SEC` (1200), `_STATE_MAX_AGE_SEC` (900), `_ERR_MAX_BYTES` (262144).  An ioreg failure is not evidence (it happens at module load, before the banner, so it can never be newer than the baseline; pm2 crash-loops it instead).  Trade-off: a replay of the last ~92 hours shows 13 of 37 outages recovered on their own in 2 to 21 minutes, and the 120s confirm will kill those; raise `_CONFIRM_SEC` toward 300 for fewer needless kills at the price of slower recovery.  Tests: `scripts/test-mac-process-watch-http.sh` and `python3 scripts/test_mac_process_watch_shellular.py` (synthetic log pairs, no kill). |

| `com.jays.claude-usage-collector` | every 15 min (`RunAtLoad`) | Claude Code session JSONL tokens -> Usage Monitor ingest (producer `claude-code`, estimated API-equivalent).  **Added Sun, Oct 25, 2026 7:00pm (OPENCODE), UM-1636.**  Reads `~/.claude/projects/**.jsonl` and sends the exact per-request usage (model, input, output, cache read, cache write) that every assistant message carries.  It exists because Claude Code's own OTLP exporter stopped emitting `claude_code.token.usage`, `claude_code.cost.usage` and the `claude_code.api_request` log event on 2026-10-23; a canary payload POSTed to production was accepted and persisted, so the receiver was healthy and the exporter was the side that stopped.  The parser was already in the Usage-Monitor repo as `scripts/claude-usage-collector.disabled.mjs`, disabled by audit #1509 because the native path used to cover the same usage; it was renamed back, shaped as a gap filler, and installed from `~/apps/usage-monitor-collectors` (`scripts/install-collectors.sh` in Usage-Monitor).  The double counting with a recovered native feed is prevented on the receiver, not by an operator promise: every event carries `metadata.collector="transcript"` plus `metadata["host.name"]`, and `/api/ingest/usage` drops a transcript event when the native feed already covers it (`src/lib/native-telemetry-coverage.ts`); the dropped events are ACKed as duplicates.  The Mac's Claude Code `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` now carry a `host.name` so that comparison can be machine-scoped, and the collector sets its own `metadata["host.name"]` from `os.hostname()`;  the two values have to match byte for byte, or the receiver stops matching the transcript events against the native feed and counts them twice.  This row does not publish the hostname value.  Three-day bootstrap lookback (the Mac holds 4,000+ transcript files), then a durable successful-through watermark in `~/.cache/usage-monitor/collector-state/claude-code.json` with a one-hour idempotent overlap (the collector passes `stateOverlapMinutes: CLAUDE_STATE_OVERLAP_MINUTES`, which is 60;  the shared 24-hour default is deliberately not used here, because re-sending a day of events every 15 minutes would push that many duplicate rows through the ingest path each time).  BotFleet-managed session paths are excluded.  Plist `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.jays.claude-usage-collector.plist`, log `~/Library/Logs/claude-usage-collector.log`.  The ingest endpoint comes from the plist's `USAGE_MONITOR_INGEST_URL` (the collector refuses to guess one;  without it every 15-minute run exits 1 and only the log shows it).   Unscoped `USAGE_INGEST_TOKEN` from Infisical Fleet-OPS root (`fleet-secrets`) until a scoped `claude-code` entry exists in `USAGE_INGEST_PRODUCER_TOKENS`.  |

| `com.jay.mac-cleanup` | **Retired 2026-10-10** (was every 4 h) | **Retired 2026-10-10; HogHunter takes over and manages these scripts directly** (owner Sun, Oct 11).  No plist, not loaded;  taken out of the `mac-process-watch.sh` expect lists Sun, Oct 11, 2026 (CLAUDE).  History below is kept for reference.  **2026-09-01 (Claude):** now takes a **re-entrant owner-token lock** (`~/.claude-disk-janitor/.housekeeper.lock`, 2h stale-steal) — it previously had none while two independent invokers reached it.  Callers already holding it export `HOUSEKEEPER_LOCK_OWNER=1` so the parent does not deadlock its own children.  `cleanmymac optimize ram` is **removed** (gated behind `RESOURCE_ALLOW_RAM_OPTIMIZE=1`): it purges resident pages into swapfiles on the same APFS container as user data, measured **+3.6-7.3G swap per run**.  Plist gained `EnvironmentVariables:PATH` — launchd's default PATH excludes `/opt/homebrew/bin`, so the `npm`/`pnpm`/`brew`/`cleanmymac` blocks had been silently no-opping under launchd.  Safe developer cache, CleanMyMac CLI (system junk, dev junk, AI cache, trash), Xcode iOS DeviceSupport/DerivedData, unavailable simulators only (`simctl delete unavailable`), agent session prune, and remote Hetzner Coolify docker prune.  **Wipes `~/.npm/_npx`.**  `--pressure` adds CleanMyMac purge + 3-day Grok session prune.  Does **not** reap git worktrees or wipe `~/.grok/worktrees` — that is `com.jay.disk-janitor`.  **2026-09-08 (Claude):** `npm cache clean --force` and `pnpm store prune` (in `~/apps/mac-auto-cleanup.sh`) now skip via `pgrep` while an `npm`/`pnpm install` is running anywhere, and the worktree dep-reap refuses to delete a `node_modules`/`.next`/`.turbo` dir that `git ls-files --error-unmatch` still shows as tracked (a sweep once deleted a tracked vendored `node_modules` under `app/vendor` in a Congress.Trade worktree).  **Sat, Sep 12, 2026 4:50pm CT (Claude):** the unconditional (every tick, no free-space gate) `cleanmymac clean --force` call no longer runs `junk` — it ran EVERY clean module including system junk, and deleted the live, launchd-held-open `~/Library/Logs/botfleet/server.log` out from under `com.jay.botfleet-server` on 2026-09-12 ~12:31 (same bug as `com.jay.disk-janitor`, PR #207).  Now runs only `cleanmymac clean dev --force`, `cleanmymac clean ai --force`, and `cleanmymac clean trash --force`; `cleanmymac purge --force` under `--pressure` and `cleanmymac optimize ram` under `RESOURCE_ALLOW_RAM_OPTIMIZE=1` are unchanged.  `~/Library/Logs`, `~/Library/Logs/botfleet`, and `~/.botfleet` are also on CleanMyMac's own ignore list now (added in PR #207, shared across every caller of the CLI on this Mac). |
| `com.jay.mac-resource-watch` | **Retired 2026-10-10** (was every 5 min) | **Retired 2026-10-10; HogHunter takes over and manages these scripts directly** (owner Sun, Oct 11).  No plist, not loaded;  taken out of the `mac-process-watch.sh` expect lists Sun, Oct 11, 2026 (CLAUDE).  History below is kept for reference.  **2026-09-01 (Claude) — loop fix.  Cleanup previously had NO cooldown (only the webhook did), so with disk hovering under the 80G warn line it ran `mac-auto-cleanup.sh --pressure` every 5 min, back to back.  Measured: each run grew swap 3.6-7.3G and 2 of 3 runs ended with LESS free disk.**  Now: `CLEAN_COOLDOWN_SEC=3600` (`900` when critical), a single-flight lock, 3x backoff when a run frees nothing, and cache cleanup only on a **disk** hit (swap/load-only pressure no longer triggers it — deleting caches cannot reduce swap).  `--clean` no longer bypasses the cooldown (all four BF-Housekeeper surfaces pass it); `--force` is the only bypass.  The `swap_total_gb >= 8` disjunct is **removed** — macOS grows the swap store on demand, so once past 8G it was true forever and every tick reported a hit; the gate is now `swap_used_pct >= 80 AND swap_used_gb >= 8`.  Webhook now sends **both** `X-BotFleet-Event` and `X-Webhook-Event` (the launchd receiver on :8800 runs older code that only reads the latter) and parses the response body — a 202 is returned for accepted/ignored/captured/duplicate alike, and only a payload carrying `runId` actually started a bot.  Samples disk free, swap, and 1-minute load.  On warn/crit (disk ≤80G / drop ≥6G / swap ≥80% and ≥8G total / load ≥16) runs `mac-auto-cleanup.sh --pressure` and POSTs BotFleet Housekeeper webhook (`resource.disk` / `resource.ram` / `resource.cpu`).  Cooldown 45 min.  Secret from Infisical `/botfleet-housekeeper-webhook` via `fleet-secrets` (never print).  Live `~/apps/mac-resource-watch.py`.  Tracked `scripts/mac-resource-watch.py` + `scripts/launchd/com.jay.mac-resource-watch.plist`. |

| `com.jay.grok-idle-unload` | hourly (3600s) | **Scheduled, not always-on.**  `session/close` on Grok chats that hold MCP tool processes under the shared leader (found with sysctl `KERN_PROCARGS2`, never logged) so those processes exit.  **Two clocks (2026-10-08, CLAUDE; AFC #380, #386, #389; installed live Thu, Oct 8, 11:50pm to Fri, Oct 9, 12:41am):** a zero-turn stub with no attached client unloads after 30 minutes idle (`GROK_IDLE_UNLOAD_STUB_MINUTES`, 0 = off), every other chat after 4 hours (`GROK_IDLE_UNLOAD_HOURS`; was 12h, and the old plist pinned 12 so a code default change did nothing).  Why: on 2026-10-08 the Grok leader held seven duplicate MCP sets (~1.6 GiB) from same-day stub sessions the 12h rule never touched, and in 45 logged runs the old `live` gate skipped every chat (nothing was ever closed).  **Idle counts from the last load too:** the clock starts at the newest of summary `updated_at`, the last turn start or end, the newest `mcp_config_resolved` / `mcp_init_completed` event and the start of the chat's current MCP processes, so a chat resumed today after a day of silence is not unloaded before its first turn.  **Attached means a grok client in the chat's directory:** the leader exposes no connection list, so a non-leader `grok` process (Shellular, BotFleet, a TUI) whose working directory equals the chat's cwd keeps it off the 30-minute clock (4 hours instead); a client whose cwd cannot be read holds every chat that run.  Limit: every stub in a directory a live client occupies (the BotFleet `c9c0e935` workspace, a TUI's folder) waits 4 hours, not 30 minutes.  **MCP versus work:** a process carrying the chat's id under the leader is an MCP server only when its branch root (the leader's child) is not a shell and was forked inside the chat's load window (just before `mcp_config_resolved` to 5 minutes after `mcp_init_completed`).  Anything else (a shell, a watcher, a process forked later) is work a tool started: it protects the chat and is never signalled, as does any process carrying the id outside the leader.  Does **not** `/delete` transcripts.  `/resume` or `grok --resume ID` reloads tools.  Skips working / needs-input / pendingTool / `$GROK_SESSION_ID` / chats with background work.  **Every close is re-checked** on a fresh process table and fresh disk state right before it runs; a chat that changed since selection is logged `changed_since_selection:<why>` and left alone.  MCP children are SIGTERMed directly only when `session/close` answers `closeOutcome: notResident` (the leader does not hold the chat but its processes still run) after one more re-check, never after a timeout, an RPC error or a refusal.  Children of sessions whose directory is gone are reaped too, at most 5 sessions a run and only with exactly one leader (the pid in `~/.grok/leader.lock`), a readable non-empty sessions directory and a successful list.  The leader's `session/list` is **not called** unless `--use-list` / `GROK_IDLE_UNLOAD_USE_LIST=1` asks for it: candidates come from the process table and disk, the list only added chats that are not loaded, and it took 14 to 90 seconds on a loaded Mac (it was the source of the recurring `helper timed out` lines).  Only `session/close` talks to the leader now, once per chat being unloaded.  Each leader helper call gets `GROK_IDLE_UNLOAD_HELPER_TIMEOUT_SEC` (180s); a timeout logs one timestamped JSON line on stderr and exits 75.  The plist no longer pins `GROK_IDLE_UNLOAD_HOURS` and runs `ProcessType Standard` (it was `Background` + `LowPriorityIO`, which starved the job at load ~300: a 4 second dry run took over 100 seconds under `taskpolicy -b`).  `launchctl kickstart` does not re-read a plist: apply a plist change with bootout then bootstrap.  Live `~/apps/grok-acp-runtime/grok-idle-unload.py` (+ `session_disk.py`, `leader-client.py`).  Tracked `scripts/grok-acp-runtime/` + `scripts/launchd/com.jay.grok-idle-unload.plist`.  On-demand: `python3 ~/apps/grok-acp-runtime/grok-idle-unload.py --dry-run --verbose`.  Tests: `python3 scripts/test_grok_idle_unload.py`. |
| `com.jay.sim-idle-reaper` | every 15 min (900s) | **Scheduled, not always-on.**  Shuts down iOS Simulators left booted and idle, for every seat (owner rule 2026-10-09:  never end a turn with a simulator booted;  a headless iPhone 17 sat booted one to two days before 2026-10-10).  Lists `xcrun simctl list devices booted -j` and SKIPS a device when an `xcodebuild`, `xctest`, `simctl` or `bitrig` process runs, or one naming an `.xctestrun` (`pgrep`, PIDs only;  a `simctl spawn <udid> log stream` does not count, because the Claude desktop app keeps one open for every booted device);  when Simulator.app is open;  when Bitrig is running (exempts up to 1 shared booted simulator for Bitrig, prioritizing the most recently seen/used device);  when a Claude session marked it watched in the iOS Simulator panel in the last 12 hours;  when it has been seen booted under 60 minutes (first-seen times in `~/Library/Application Support/sim-idle-reaper/state.json`, since simctl exposes no boot time);  or when a User app (`simctl listapps`) is running in it (`simctl spawn <udid> launchctl list`;  a failed check protects the device).  Otherwise `xcrun simctl shutdown <udid>`:  never `shutdown all`, never erase or delete.  TestFlight ships archive for `generic/platform=iOS` and boot no simulator.  One log line per decision;  a lock file makes overlapping runs exit;  it also prunes the Claude hook's stale state.  `RunAtLoad` false, so a bootstrap never shuts anything down;  `ProcessType Standard`.  Logs `~/Library/Logs/sim-idle-reaper.log` (+ `.err.log`).  Live `~/apps/sim-idle-reaper.py`;  tracked `scripts/sim-idle-reaper.py` + `scripts/launchd/com.jay.sim-idle-reaper.plist`, installed with `python3 scripts/install-sim-idle-reaper.py` (board `e1af87aa`).  Kept loaded by `mac-process-watch.sh` (`expect_scheduled`).  On-demand:  `python3 ~/apps/sim-idle-reaper.py --dry-run --verbose`;  `--udid <UDID>` limits a run to one device.  Tests:  `python3 scripts/test_sim_idle_reaper.py`.  Added Sat, Oct 10, 2026 7:18am (CLAUDE). |
| `com.jay.fleet-work-backup` | every 3 h (10800s) | **Retired 2026-10-19 (owner ruling):  never installed on this Mac, zero snapshots, B2 bucket `jays-fleet-work-backup` left in place and empty;  code stays in the repo.**  Older note:  **Installed, pending owner steps** (Sun, Oct 11, 2026):  the script is installed at `~/apps/fleet-work-backup/fleet_work_backup.py`, and the LaunchAgent is not in `~/Library/LaunchAgents` or loaded until Jay finishes the owner steps, so it is not running.  Scheduled, not always-on.  Bucket `jays-fleet-work-backup` (owner-confirmed Oct 11).  Backs up what GitHub does not hold to restic on Backblaze B2 (repo `fleet-work-backup/` in the `B2_FLEET_BUCKET_NAME` bucket, tag `fleet-work`):  per repository `git bundle` files of the branches no network remote has (bounded by the remote's default branch, so a branch GitHub later deletes cannot break a restore), every stash and, per worktree, the uncommitted work (with `anchors.bundle` holding the commits those patches apply to when GitHub may not keep them) (one patch per tracked text file, a changed binary file whole, plus untracked non-ignored files), and the non-git files under `~/apps` (protocol docs, effort logs, helper scripts, `mac-collab/findings.db` via the SQLite backup API).  Secrets are never staged:  a name filter before copy, then a regex scan (binary files included) and `gitleaks dir` when installed over the staged set drop matches and log paths only;  with gitleaks installed the gate fails closed (a text file it has not cleared waits for the next run, shown as `deferred`).  Owner reason (Sat, Oct 10, 2026):  Time Machine and `/Volumes/External` are the same SSD, and Time Machine never covered `~/apps`.  Skips a run when load1 is over 60, memory pressure is critical, or swap is at 95 percent while pressure is at least warning (a `flock` also stops overlap);  a warning appears after 8 consecutive skips or runs that made no complete snapshot, and then the job posts once to Zulip (`simple-chat post --as CLAUDE`, topic `AFC 29c02bcb fleet-work-backup`, at most daily while it lasts, recipients `FLEET_WORK_BACKUP_ALERT_TO`).  `forget` after every complete run (never after a partial one), `--prune` at most daily, `restic check` at most weekly.  `RunAtLoad` false, `ProcessType Standard`, `Nice 5`, `LowPriorityIO`.  **TCC:**  launchd children cannot read `/Volumes/External`, so the run is `status=partial warn=removable-volumes-denied grant=<executable>` (snapshot tagged `partial`) until Removable Volumes is granted to the interpreter's running `Python.app` (`fleet_work_backup.py image` prints it;  the interpreter is Apple's `/usr/bin/python3`, whose signature keeps the grant across updates, where Homebrew's ad-hoc one loses it on every upgrade);  `check-access --launchd` verifies it with a one-shot launchd job, not Terminal.  Logs `~/Library/Logs/fleet-work-backup.log` (one line per run) and `.detail.log` (dropped paths).  Live `~/apps/fleet-work-backup/fleet_work_backup.py`;  tracked `scripts/fleet-work-backup/` + `scripts/launchd/com.jay.fleet-work-backup.plist`, installed with `python3 scripts/fleet-work-backup/install.py`.  Credentials by name only:  `RESTIC_FLEET_WORK_PASSWORD`, `B2_FLEET_WORK_KEY_ID`, `B2_FLEET_WORK_APPLICATION_KEY` (Infisical `AI Fleet Coordinator` prod, copied to the handoff file by `com.jay.infisical-secrets-sync`).  Restore: `scripts/fleet-work-backup/RESTORE.md`.  Added Sat, Oct 10, 2026 4:15pm (CLAUDE);  reworked in review Sat, Oct 10, 2026 6:50pm (CLAUDE, PR #454);  review fixes Sat, Oct 10, 2026 9:48pm (CLAUDE, branch `claude/fleet-work-backup-p1`). |

Tracked in AFC (Sun, Oct 11, 2026, CLAUDE, Linear AFC-286, open-loops unit C6-2);  not loaded until live step L16.**  The fallback notifier for needs-owner items.  LaunchAgent `scripts/launchd/com.jay.needs-owner-notify.plist` runs `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3 -I ~/apps/needs-owner/needs-owner notify` as CLAUDE (`AGENT_SEAT=CLAUDE`, no launcher), `StartInterval` 300, `RunAtLoad` false, `ProcessType` Standard, `Nice` 5, PATH with `~/.local/bin`.  Each run reads the open items from Linear (a failed read sends nothing and exits 0) and: posts a notice signed CLAUDE "for <SEAT>" for an item still `notify_pending` after 2 minutes (urgent first);  records the owner's answer as one Linear comment `owner replied:` (a tap on a low-stakes option from the pinned owner id, a message `agent-sync verify-owner-msg` passes, or a Linear comment by `OWNER_LINEAR_USER_ID` that is not an app's) and wakes the filing seat with one directed note (`simple-chat post --to <SEAT>`), or for CLAUDE's own items writes `~/apps/needs-owner/state/pending-CLAUDE.json`;  asks again once for two different taps and once for a tap on a risky option;  mirrors a Grok Bot persona's `needs-owner-request` block into Linear.  It never closes or restates an item.  Every post counts against `NEEDS_OWNER_CAPS` (3 urgent an hour, 6 an hour, 30 a day) and the pause is `NEEDS_OWNER_NOTIFY_PAUSED` (Infisical) or Jay's verified `pause` in any OWNER topic (24 hours or until `resume`).  Reads the K1 knobs through `fleet_knobs` (the stable copy carries it) and talks to `board` and `agent-sync` as child processes;  no secret is read, printed or logged.  State, lock and the file for CLAUDE's items live in `~/apps/needs-owner/state/` (mode 0700;  `notify-state.json`, `notify.lock` with a 30 minute stale steal, `pending-CLAUDE.json`;  ids and numbers only).  Log `~/Library/Logs/needs-owner-notify.log` (ids and reason words only). Pause now:  `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.needs-owner-notify`, or set `NEEDS_OWNER_NOTIFY_PAUSED` in Infisical.  Tests:  `cd scripts && python3 -m unittest discover -s needs_owner/tests -t .`.  Contract: `docs/protocols/needs-owner.md`. |
| `com.jay.mac-facts-publish` | every 3 hours (12:30, 3:30, 6:30 and 9:30, am and pm) | **Always-on (kept loaded by `mac-process-watch.sh`, `expect_scheduled`;  idle between runs).  Tracked in AFC (Sun, Oct 11, 2026, CLAUDE, Linear AFC-285, open-loops unit F7);  not loaded until live step L16c, after L4, L5 and L15.**  Publishes the Mac's open-loops facts, signed, to the PRIVATE repository `Simple-With-Us/fleet-loops-data`.  LaunchAgent `scripts/launchd/com.jay.mac-facts-publish.plist` runs the dedicated launcher `~/apps/fleet-facts/mac-facts-publish` (installed by `scripts/fleet_facts/install.sh --apply`, so a Removable Volumes grant can be scoped to that one file;  never Full Disk Access) as CLAUDE (`AGENT_SEAT=CLAUDE`, no launcher variable), eight `StartCalendarInterval` entries, `RunAtLoad` false, `ProcessType` Standard, `Nice` 5, `LowPriorityIO`.  Each run takes a lock (a second copy exits), starts a 20-minute cap that also ends its child processes, skips at load1 over 250 (the Infisical knobs `FLEET_FACTS_PUBLISH_CAP_MINUTES` and `FLEET_FACTS_PUBLISH_MAX_LOAD1`, defaults 20 and 250, which a local setting can only lower;  the files already published stay and the status file marks them stale;  their `generated_at` is never refreshed), fetches `FLEET_FACTS_SIGNING_KEY` through K1 (Infisical, never cached or printed;  no key means nothing is published), gathers the lane doctor report, the C1 status, the P1 backup health and the needs-owner list, builds the full list with `open-loops gather` and `render --markdown` (skipped and reported when open-loops is not installed), and writes `gb/full-list.md` (a short not-available note when the list could not be built), `gb/mac-facts.json` (no paths, quotes cut to 80 characters) and, only when the Infisical knob `FLEET_FACTS_PUBLISH_FULL_COPY` is on (off by default, because it keeps lane paths and Grok Bot's token can read the whole repository;  while it is off every run removes the file from the branch first thing if an earlier run left one, before the key, the load check or the repository gate), `mac-facts.json` through sha-aware `gh api -X PUT` calls (no clone, never forced;  the one delete it can make is that file's).  If the facts write fails after the new list landed, the list is replaced by the not-available note.  Before any write it refuses unless the repository is PRIVATE with no webhook, no deploy key and no installed app that can read it (apps the owner accepted are named in the Infisical knob `FLEET_LOOPS_DATA_ALLOWED_APPS`, empty by default), and it withholds everything if a secret-shaped string survives per-field redaction.  Each JSON file carries an HMAC-SHA256 `sig`;  `fleet-facts verify FILE` checks it with `FLEET_FACTS_SIGNING_KEY` in the environment.  State, lock and dry-run output live in `~/.local/state/fleet-facts/` (mode 0700;  `mac-facts-publish.json` holds the last result, file results, counts and reason words only).  Log `~/Library/Logs/mac-facts-publish.log` (ids, counts and reason words only).  Install:  `scripts/fleet_facts/install.sh --apply` (the stable copy, which now carries `fleet_knobs`), copy the plist to `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`, then `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.jay.mac-facts-publish.plist`.  Try it without sending:  `fleet-facts publish-mac --dry-run`.  Pause now:  `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.mac-facts-publish`.  Tests:  `cd scripts && python3 -m unittest discover -s fleet_facts/tests -t .`. |

| `com.jay.hetzner-backups-prune` | every 6 h, :30 local (03/09/15/21) | **Added Sat, Oct 4, 2026 (BF-PLUMBER), board `493a525f`.**  Keeps the keep-two backup plan on Coolify host `fleet-hetzner-nbg1` honest **without depending on a BF-Housekeeper wake** — the prune used to run only as a side effect of a Housekeeper check, so any missed wake silently cost ~15 GiB (2026-10-04: host went 183G → 198G, 69% full, because a third group landed in `/data/backups/socratic` + `/data/backups/congress` and nothing pruned them; Housekeeper applied the prune by hand, back to 182G).  Live `~/apps/hk-backups-prune/` — `hk-backups-scheduled.sh` (wrapper, the only thing launchd calls), plus BF-HOUSEKEEPER's original `hk-backups-prune.sh` + `hk-backups-plan.sh` copied **byte-identical** (sha256 `2c6d21de…` / `598c490f…`; they resolve each other via `dirname $0`, so they must stay side by side).  Plist `~/apps/hk-backups-prune/com.jay.hetzner-backups-prune.plist`.  Log `~/Library/Logs/hk-backups-prune.log` (5 MiB self-truncate) + `.launchd.{out,err}.log`.  **Scheduled, not always-on.  Never repoint this at `prune-apply` directly.**  Order is: dry run → gate → apply → re-verify, and the gates are (a) **20-min in-flight guard** — refuses to touch anything if the newest backup file is younger than 20 min, because a socratic group takes ~12 min to land (`.db` then `.sha256`) and pruning under a writing backup is the one way this could hurt; (b) `PLAN_DONE apply=0` must be present or the plan is untrusted; (c) every `OLD` path must sit under an allow-listed root (`/data/backups/{socratic,congress,usage-monitor,qdrant/socratic-trade,fleet-agents}/`) with no `..`; (d) caps — max 12 files and max 3 pending groups per dir, so a backup outage pages instead of deleting a pile.  `rm` note: the `rm -f` in `hk-backups-plan.sh` runs on the **remote** host (the plan is piped to `bash -s` over ssh), so the Mac's mavis-trash shim never sees it.  The only Mac-local key cleanup is the `find -depth -delete` + `SCRUB_VERIFIED` trap in `hk-backups-prune.sh` — **do not rewrite it as `rm -rf`**, that is how a private key ends up in `~/.Trash`.  The wrapper itself calls no `rm` at all.  Cadence is deliberately ~2h15m **after** the observed backup window (:15 past 01:15/07:15 Central, from the 06:15Z/12:15Z group stamps) so it never races a backup.  `RunAtLoad: false` on purpose — installing or re-bootstrapping must never be the thing that deletes.  Creds come from Infisical (`COOLIFY_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY`, project `18f563a3…`, env `prod`) via `INFISICAL_AUTOMATION_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` in Infisical Fleet-OPS root (`fleet-secrets`); the plist sets `PATH` (launchd's default omits `/opt/homebrew/bin`, where `infisical` lives) and `HOME`.  **Known gap: this job is silent on failure** — it only writes its own log.  An alert needs a new producer token in Infisical `USAGE_INGEST_PRODUCER_TOKENS` plus a Usage Monitor redeploy, which was out of scope for this change; until then a lapsed prune is only caught by disk pressure.  `.janitor-keep` sits in the dir because `com.jay.disk-janitor`'s `KEEP_RE` does not list it.  Disable: `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.hetzner-backups-prune`.  Verified 2026-10-04: 10/10 gate tests against canned plans (traversal, outside-roots, cap breaches, missing `PLAN_DONE`, helper rc≠0, mid-write skip, happy path, still-pending-after-apply), plus a real `launchctl kickstart` → `runs = 1`, `last exit code = 0`, keep-two already holding, apply never reached. |

| Name | Kind | What it is |

| `com.jay.infisical-secrets-sync` | every 900 s (15 min) | **Was unlisted; row added Sun, Oct 4, 2026 (MINIMAX).**  One-way sync of designated Infisical secrets **into** `~/.secrets/global-api-keys` — Infisical is the source of truth, that file is a derived convenience copy for the many tools that still read it.  `~/bin/infisical-secrets-sync.sh` (live), plist `RunAtLoad: true` + `StartInterval: 900`, logs to `~/.secrets/infisical-sync.log`.  **Retires last** (AFC-359):  `scripts/retire-secrets-dir.sh bootout` boots it out, disables it and moves its plist, script and `~/apps/infisical-sync` to `~/apps/_retired/infisical-secrets-sync-<stamp>/` with a rollback note;  the cutover `check` fails while it is loaded.  Nothing reads the file it writes.  Needs the Infisical CLI authenticated in the **GUI session** (keychain login token), so it does nothing useful headless.  Adding a key means appending `LOCAL_KEY\|PROJECT_ID\|ENV\|SECRET_NAME` to the script's `MAP` array — one row per synced key.  Currently maps a single key (`CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`) out of the shared project `18f563a3…`, and it reads env **`prod`** (Infisical's only environment;  switched from `dev` Sat, Oct 10, 2026 (CLAUDE), and the script now refuses any other ENV).  Writes a backup per run and prunes to the 5 most recent `global-api-keys.bak-sync-*`.  Refuses to write a value it considers implausible (shorter than 16 chars, or equal to the secret name) and logs a warning instead — so a malformed fetch degrades to "no change" rather than corrupting the handoff file.  **Failure diagnosed Sat, Oct 10, 2026 (CLAUDE):**  every tick since Sep 29 logged `WARN: fetch failed or implausible … (got_len=3)` although `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` exists in shared prod (and dev, same 53 characters).  The old code took the last field of the CLI's default table, which is now a 3-byte box-drawing border.  The fixed script reads `--plain --silent`, keeps the length and no-space checks, and logs lengths only.  Tracked source `scripts/infisical-secrets-sync.sh`, plist `scripts/launchd/com.jay.infisical-secrets-sync.plist`, hermetic test `scripts/test-infisical-secrets-sync.sh`;  install with `install -m 755 scripts/infisical-secrets-sync.sh ~/bin/` then bootout and bootstrap.  A working tick writes the real value into `global-api-keys` (that is the job);  no value is invented.  Live install Sat, Oct 10, 2026 6:23am (CLAUDE):  old script and plist backed up to `~/bin/*.bak-20261010-062308`, merged script installed, bootout then bootstrap;  the first tick logged `updated CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN (new_len=53)` with no WARN, and the handoff file now holds the key (length only checked).  Scheduled, not KeepAlive.  Disable: `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.infisical-secrets-sync`. |
