## On-demand helpers (able to be run; must not stay up)

These are the installed scripts agents actually invoke.  They are **not**
daemons.  If you add another one that other seats should use, list it here.
Lanes (`~/apps/lanes/<Repo>/<seat>-<slug>`, the earlier `~/apps/lanes/<prefix>/` folders, and the legacy flat
`~/apps/congress-grok-*`, `trading-grok-*`) are **not** listed — those die with the
branch.

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `~/apps/mcode-remote-sessions.py` | On-demand.  Lists and reads MiniMax Code remote-project sessions.  A "remote project" in the MiniMax Code desktop app is an SSH project, and a thread started on one runs on the REMOTE host's own mcode runtime, so it never appears in the Mac's `mavis session list`, `mcode --session`, or the Mac's `runtime-state.sqlite`.  The desktop app keeps the registry in `~/Library/Application Support/MiniMax Agent/ssh-connections.json` and `ssh-projects.json`.  Usage: `hosts` (the app's SSH connections and projects), `list <host-alias>` (that host's projects and their sessions with child sessions), `read <host-alias> <session-id> [count]` (the last messages of one remote thread), `resume <host-alias> <session-id>`.  It copies itself over SSH and runs on the host, talking JSON-RPC over WebSocket to the host's `~/.minimax/app-server-control/app-server.sock` inside `desktopService/transport`, so it needs no local app-server.  Reads only, except the explicit `resume`.  Added Oct 25, 2026 (MM).  No daemon and no schedule. |
| `~/apps/gapcheck.sh` | On-demand.  Checks a file for the house sentence gap:  a visibly wider gap after `.`, `!` and `?`, and never after a colon, semicolon, comma or dash.  Usage `./gapcheck.sh <file> [nbsp or ascii]`, where `nbsp` is a GitHub or Zulip surface and `ascii` is a file, a commit message or a terminal.  It reports the gap count, any sentence break that still carries a single space, a gap after a forbidden mark, an `&nbsp;` entity in a nbsp surface, and an em dash.  It exists because the house presence check `grep -o` with the empty bracket class returns 0 on macOS:  BSD grep rejects that class, so a converted body and an unconverted one look identical.  Added Oct 21, 2026 (MM).  No daemon and no schedule. |
| `muster/script/build_and_run.sh` (AI-Fleet-Coordinator, run from an owned lane) | On-demand.  Generates the XcodeGen project, selects a Developer ID Application identity, builds the Muster Mac app, verifies its signature and runtime settings, and installs one copy at `~/Applications/Muster.app`.  Uses PID-only discovery plus bounded executable-text inspection and refuses uninspectable active processes.  Refuses a running-app replacement unless `--relaunch` is explicit; Debug uses a distinct bundle ID and staging is removed after install.  No daemon or schedule.  Added Mon, Oct 19, 2026 (CODEX, AFC-388). |
| Socratic-Trade `scripts/install-fleet-coordination.sh`, `scripts/setup-slack-sync.sh`, retired `scripts/agent-sync-bootstrap.sh`, and cloud setup entry points | On-demand through setup; installation is Linux/cloud only, and Mac execution retires old Slack hooks before skipping installation.  No daemon or schedule.  Updated Oct 12, 2026 (CODEX, CTS-330, PR #4523).  Installs pinned stdlib Linear/Zulip clients plus their registry and protocol in the cloud user cache and bin directories, requires Python 3.11, and retires only persisted Slack SessionStart entries and the copied retired engine.  Preserves unrelated settings; malformed JSON or symlinks block cleanup.  New cache pins are extracted into clean sibling staging directories and pass offline CLI import checks before replacing the previous cache.  Runtime credentials and identity verification remain separate prerequisites; no secret provisioning, model session, or live Mac settings change.  Nine isolated tests and earlier real pinned-source offline import/registry smoke checks passed.  The former self-contained bootstrap now fails closed and cannot reinstall the Slack engine or hook.  PR #4523 review and hosted gates remain pending. |
| `~/Code/ai-fleet-coordinator/scripts/{build-fleet-daily-digest.py,build-agent-calendar.py}` | On-demand locally and scheduled in hosted GitHub Actions every 6 hours.  Rebuilds the public activity HTML, Markdown, and ICS feeds from repositories explicitly allowlisted in `scripts/public_activity_repos.py` and verified currently public through GitHub API.  Internal effort boards are never read into public feeds.  No Mac daemon or local schedule is installed.  Updated 2026-09-26 (CODEX). |
| `clutch-runtime/scripts/lib/clutch-tailnet-host.sh` (tracked source in `Simple-With-Us/Clutch`; sourced, never run directly) | Transient, self-started, never stays up.  Added 2026-10-11 (MINIMAX, board `7d6fd204`, PR #108).  `clutch_tailnet_host` does the one MagicDNS read that `start-web.sh` and `serve-tailscale.sh` share; `clutch_reconcile_tailnet_host` is forked detached by `start-web.sh` **only** when the read came back empty, and exits after at most one corrective `pm2 restart clutch-web`, or after `CLUTCH_TAILNET_RECONCILE_SECONDS` (default 1800).  It restarts only after `clutch_running_trusts_host` confirms the live process is actually missing the host, and never more than `CLUTCH_TAILNET_MAX_RESTARTS` (default 3).  **Why it exists:**  macOS starts Tailscale minutes after login, so a single boot-time read could return nothing and leave the server trusting loopback only, which 403s every `/api` request from the Clutch iOS app until someone restarts by hand.  Set `CLUTCH_TAILNET_HOST` to bypass detection and skip the reconciler entirely.  Knobs: `CLUTCH_TAILNET_WAIT_SECONDS` (30), `CLUTCH_TAILNET_POLL_SECONDS` (15), `CLUTCH_TAILNET_RECONCILE_SECONDS` (1800), `CLUTCH_TAILNET_MAX_RESTARTS` (3). | Not running when healthy;  spawns transiently at boot only |
| `~/apps/otel-lane-tag.sh` (tracked source `scripts/otel-lane-tag.sh`, test `scripts/test-otel-lane-tag.sh`; copy the tracked file over the live one after a merge) | On-demand.  Tags a Claude Code lane's untracked `.claude/settings.local.json` with `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES=seat=<seat>,lane=<dir-name>,project=<repo>,repo=<repo>` so its sessions carry seat/lane/app attribution into Sentry `agent-sessions` (`project=` also drives Usage Monitor's per-project cost attribution — see UM `AGENTS.md` "Per-project cost attribution").  Reads ai-fleet-coordinator's `fleet-apps.json` from `origin/main` (never `~/Code`) for the prefix → repo and seat → worktreeSuffix mapping.  Run it after creating a new lane, or `--all` to sweep every lane under `~/apps` once (safe to re-run; skips anything under `~/Code`, anything not a git worktree, and any lane whose name does not parse as `<prefix>-<seat>[-suffix]`, and leaves an existing `.envrc` project value alone).  Only ever touches that one untracked file and, inside it, only the `env.OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` key.  **Verified 2026-09-24 (Claude):** Sentry's OTLP log ingest reserves the top-level `project` key and silently overwrites an incoming resource attribute of the same name with its own project slug — the same collision already seen for `organization.id`.  `repo=` is the Sentry-safe duplicate of the same value; `project=` stays because that is the exact key Usage Monitor's OTLP-metrics project-resolver reads, and metrics do not go through this collision.  Added 2026-09-24 (Claude).  **Layout v2 (2026-10-09, CLAUDE):** the folder above a lane is the repo's `~/Code` folder name, so the script maps it back to the worktree prefix through `fleet-apps.json` and the telemetry `lane=` name keeps its `<prefix>-<seat>-<slug>` shape; a Claude desktop worktree (`<slug>-<6 hex>`) is tagged `seat=claude`; `review-pr-<n>`, `_codex`, `_managed`, `_review` and the symlinks the migration leaves are skipped.  Now tracked in the repo instead of live-only.  **Sat, Oct 10, 2026 8:49am (CLAUDE, AFC #449):**  a real path on the external lanes disk (`/Volumes/External/Lanes/...`, setting `FLEET_LANES_EXTERNAL_ROOT`) is read at its `~/apps/lanes` spelling, so a shell parked in an external lane tags it like any other.  Live copy refreshed from main (bb9a327fbc40);  backup `~/apps/_archive/2026-10-10/otel-lane-tag.sh.bak-extlanes`. |

The value comes from the environment,  from `fleet-secrets get`,  or from a 0600 file the owner handed over;  the GitHub CLI's own keychain entry and a generic keychain lookup are not sources,  because the keychain is reserved for the machine identity.  |  |

| `~/.claude/hooks/command-class-pretooluse.py` | Always-on (Claude Code PreToolUse hook, matcher `Bash`, registered in `~/.claude/settings.json` alongside `board-check.sh` and `secret-guard-pretooluse.py`).  Idle-poll detection signal: classifies each Bash command into a fixed enum (`poll-wait` / `git` / `test` / `build` / `deploy` / `read` / `other`) in-process from the command text only, then emits exactly one OTLP log record `{class, session.id, seat}` to the existing Sentry `agent-sessions` logs pipeline — fire-and-forget, ~1.2 s socket timeout, never the command text or any other field.  Never blocks the tool call or changes the permission decision: always exits 0 with no stdout, and any network/parse failure is silently swallowed.  `seat` is read from `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` (see `otel-lane-tag.sh` above), falling back to `AGENT_SEAT`.  The OTLP destination (`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_ENDPOINT` / `_HEADERS`) is read directly from `~/.claude/settings.json` rather than assumed to be inherited from the parent process's env — **verified 2026-09-24 (Claude):** a live `claude -p` run showed the parent session's own `claude_code.*` logs reaching Sentry fine while this hook's log did not arrive, confirming a PreToolUse hook subprocess is not guaranteed to inherit the parent's `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_*` env; reading the settings file directly fixed it, re-verified live in-session afterward (`poll-wait`, `git`, `build`, `read`, `other` all confirmed arriving with correct `class`/`seat`/`session.id`).  Added 2026-09-24 (Claude). |
| `~/apps/usage-monitor-collectors/scripts/antigravity-statusline-telemetry.mjs` | On-demand — invoked by `agy` itself, not by an agent.  Configured 2026-09-23 (CLAUDE) as the Antigravity CLI's `statusLine` command in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` (`"statusLine": {"command": "/opt/homebrew/bin/node ~/apps/usage-monitor-collectors/scripts/antigravity-statusline-telemetry.mjs", "stack_with_default": true}`, backup `settings.json.bak-statusline-20260923`)  The command was repointed to the collectors copy when the collector scripts moved to `~/apps/usage-monitor-collectors/scripts/`;  this row was corrected Oct 28, 2026 (MM) to match the live setting..  `stack_with_default: true` keeps the owner's normal built-in status line unchanged — the sink prints nothing to stdout, it only reads agy's status JSON on stdin and appends model/token counters to `~/.cache/usage-monitor/antigravity-statusline/usage.jsonl` for `com.jays.antigravity-session-collector` (Scheduled, above) to forward.  No prompts, paths, transcripts, or tool data are ever written.  Did not touch `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json` (owner's `moshi-hook` entry). |
| `~/.local/share/agent-hook-otlp/agent-hook-otlp.mjs` (pinned copy; source `scripts/agent-hook-otlp.mjs` in Usage-Monitor) | On-demand — shared hook-to-OTLP shim for the three coding-agent CLIs that have a native hook mechanism but no OTel exporter of their own: agy (`~/.gemini/config/hooks.json`, `PostInvocation`/`Stop` only — `PostToolUse` is NOT a recognized agy hook event in the installed CLI build, confirmed via `agy -p "/hooks" --output-format json` with and without an added `matcher` key, so it is not wired; `moshi-hook`'s own `PreInvocation`/`Stop` entries are alongside it, restored 2026-09-25 after a 2026-09-24T19:35 edit had silently dropped `moshi-hook`'s `Stop` entry), Cursor (`~/.cursor/hooks.json`, `afterFileEdit`/`afterAgentResponse`/`stop`, alongside `moshi-hook` and `mac-process-check.sh`), and GitHub Copilot CLI (`~/.copilot/hooks/agent-hook-otlp.json`, `postToolUse`/`sessionEnd`/`errorOccurred` — confirmed native support in Copilot CLI 1.0.68 by its own `[WARNING] postToolUse hook from ".../agent-hook-otlp.json"` log line; wiring present, but as of 2026-09-25 only `postToolUse` has actually fired live — `sessionEnd`/`errorOccurred` are unconfirmed live, not necessarily broken).  **Copilot is a supported platform again (owner Tue, Oct 20, 2026), but `~/.copilot/hooks/agent-hook-otlp.json` was lost with the old `~/.copilot` and is not rewired:  the Oct 21 reinstall wrote only the lane guard, so this shim covers only agy and Cursor until someone re-adds the Copilot entry**;  the Copilot wiring in the source is kept for that.  Claude Code and Codex CLI already export OTLP natively and are not wired through this script.  Reads one hook payload on stdin, maps it through a strict allowlist (`agent.platform`, `seat`, `event`, `tool.name`, `success`, `duration_ms`, `session.id`, `model` — never prompt text, tool arguments, file paths, or commands) to one OTLP log record, and hands it to a DETACHED background `node ... --send` child for the actual POST to Sentry `agent-sessions` (same OTLP/HTTP JSON logs endpoint and `x-sentry-auth` header Claude Code uses) — the foreground hook process itself never awaits the network and returns as soon as the send is scheduled.  **Why detached (2026-09-25 fix):** the prior foreground-`await` design let a cold Node start alone (2-3.85 s observed on this Mac under heavy concurrent-agent load) exceed the 3 s host hook timeout; agy's own CLI logs showed 44 hook kills across two bursts on 2026-09-24, each dropping a telemetry record and stalling the host's hook wait for the full timeout.  Credentials resolve from env vars or a chmod-600 `~/.config/usage-monitor/agent-hook-otlp-sentry.json` (regeneration one-liner in the script's own header comment; re-tightens the file to 0600 even if it pre-existed).  `postOtlp` (now running inside the detached child) only ever sends to an `https://` URL whose host is `sentry.io` or a subdomain of it (`isTrustedSentryEndpoint`), independent of the credentials file's own content.  Always exits 0; a misconfigured or unreachable Sentry endpoint never blocks the host tool or fails the hook — Antigravity's wired events specifically need the fixed `{}` no-op reply (not `{"decision":"continue"}`) so the hook does not block termination.  **Pinned install path (2026-09-25 fix):** every hooks.json above points at this stable path, never at a path inside `~/Code/Usage-Monitor` — that tree is reset by a daemon and can be checked out on any branch at any time (it was on an unrelated branch when this was caught), so a hook pointed directly at it would silently break (`MODULE_NOT_FOUND`) or regress to a since-fixed bug depending on what happened to be checked out.  Refresh the pinned copy after any change to `scripts/agent-hook-otlp.mjs` on `origin/main` with `node scripts/install-agent-hook-otlp.mjs` (reads the file's content from `origin/main` via `git show`, not from the working tree; prints only the dest path and byte count).  Verified live end-to-end in Sentry for all three platforms (`cursor.afterFileEdit`, `antigravity.Stop`/`PostInvocation`, `copilot.postToolUse`) with only allowlisted fields present.  Tests: `scripts/__tests__/agent-hook-otlp.test.mjs`, `scripts/__tests__/install-agent-hook-otlp.test.mjs`.  Landed jaywedgeworth22/Usage-Monitor #1535, hardened in #1540 (Sentry-only endpoint allowlist), #1542 (Antigravity Stop no-op reply), and (detached background send, pinned install path, agy `PostToolUse` removal, `moshi-hook` `Stop` restoration) #1547.  Added 2026-09-25 (Claude), updated 2026-09-25 (Claude, land-stage adversarial-review fixes). |

| `~/apps/apple-notes-coding.sh` | Create/update/pin Coding notes.  On-demand.  **The live copy is installed from `scripts/apple-notes-coding.sh` in the AI-Fleet-Coordinator repository, and it is now that file byte for byte;  after any change to the repository copy, reinstall it here, because a fix that lives only in this copy is a fix that comes back (AFC-436).**  Fixed Thu, Sep 17, 2026: non-ASCII titles no longer duplicate on --update.  **Sun, Oct 19, 2026 (MM, AFC-403):**  a failed body conversion no longer skips the note's scratch cleanup, and the cleanup leaves nothing behind in the note body.  The writer's `Session:` row is the **third** row, a small `Session: <id>` directly under the timestamp with no repeated date;  a chat tag such as `session8` is refused as a session id.  **Tue, Oct 20, 2026 (MM):**  the headless pin and unpin open the Shortcuts app in the background first.  Its "Find Notes" action is an app intent served by the Shortcuts app, so with that app closed the shortcut failed for every title, including one that does not exist, with "The action 'Find Notes' could not run because an unknown error occurred", and no note was ever pinned.  The app is opened with `open -g`, so it does not steal focus.  **Tue, Oct 20, 2026 (MM, AFC-431):**  a failed pin run is retried once, 3 s later, so a race against the note index no longer leaves a coding note unpinned, and the retry is gated on the `APPLE_NOTES_PIN_RETRY` fleet knob, which the environment can only narrow and never widen.  A failed run, a Shortcut that is not installed, a Shortcut list that cannot be read, and pinning disabled on purpose are four different problems and are now reported as four.  **Tue, Oct 20, 2026 (MM, AFC-436):**  the two Notes calls carry a `with timeout of 900 seconds` wrapper, because a large body takes longer than AppleScript's two-minute default and the write fails with "AppleEvent timed out. (-1712)".  The value is validated as a whole number of seconds before it is used,  so a bad override cannot abort the write,  and the Shortcut run and the Shortcut list read are each bounded,  so a hung Shortcut cannot outlive the caller's cap.    **Tue, Oct 20, 2026 (MM, CC-177):**  two more fixes in the same helper.  The code-span style was written as a Python raw string, so the escaped quotes reached the HTML literally and the attribute never parsed, which also made a large body three times its size;  the quotes are real now and a code span renders as the intended monospace chip.  And the `--update` path had its `with timeout of ${NOTES_APPLE_EVENT_TIMEOUT_S} seconds` inside a quoted heredoc, so the variable never expanded and AppleScript refused to parse the script, which broke every update and every pin;  the heredoc is unquoted now and the update path works again.  The master list note could not be refreshed for a third reason: Notes refused every body operation on that one note, read, write and delete alike, while a new note with the same content wrote fine, so it was renamed to "superseded" and replaced by a fresh note built from this file.  **Wed, Oct 21, 2026 (MM, AFC-448):**  the caller starts the helper in its own session,  and a timeout kills the whole process group,  so a killed helper no longer leaves a stuck `shortcuts` child behind,  and any other interruption takes the group down with it.  The managed runtime picks the caller change up on its own:  `com.jay.agent-sync-runtime-sync` checks out origin/main every 120 s.  Always-on?  No: on-demand, and the Shortcuts app is left running afterwards.  **Wed, Oct 21, 2026 (MM):**  the live copy was behind the repository copy again and was reinstalled from AI-Fleet-Coordinator `main` `scripts/apple-notes-coding.sh`, byte for byte, which brought in the AFC #804 fix: the pin retry now passes the bare topic (the `[TEAM_KEY, Agent]` prefix and any leading non-alphanumeric run stripped) instead of resending the identical full title, because the Shortcut's Find Notes action matches by contains and a retry that re-sends the same string can only ever fix a race.  The gap showed up as a note left unpinned after `pinned: the 'Pin Coding Note' shortcut ran but did not complete`, with the owner looking at an unpinned note.  The copy had drifted by about 1.8 KB of unrelated fix work, and the reinstall brought every one of those changes back with it.  Always-on?  No: on-demand.  **Wed, Oct 21, 2026 (CLAUDE, AFC-454, AI-Fleet-Coordinator #814):**  the GUI pin fallback is gone.  When the "Pin Coding Note" Shortcut did not pin, the helper used to bring Notes to the front and click its Pin menu item, which moved keyboard focus into Notes five times between 7:07pm and 7:08pm on Tue, Oct 20.  Now a failed pin leaves the note saved unpinned with `pin skipped: ... note saved unpinned` on stderr (create and update still exit 0, `--pin-only` exits 4), and nothing brings Notes forward except an explicit `--activate` or `APPLE_NOTES_ACTIVATE=1`.  `--pin` and `APPLE_NOTES_PIN=1` are still accepted and change nothing.  Only one pin or unpin Shortcut runs at a time across processes (a lock directory at `~/Library/Caches/apple-notes-coding/pin.lock`, waiting up to `NOTES_PIN_LOCK_WAIT_S`, default 20 s, then skipping the pin), and a Shortcut run that hit its 30 s bound is not repeated, because a hang is a picker waiting on the owner's screen and a second run only opens a second one.  Callers that only need the write can set `APPLE_NOTES_PIN=0`.  The live copy was reinstalled from `main` (merge `cf5d48d9`) at 5:45am.  Follow-up #818 (merge `230104fe`, reinstalled at 7:07am, byte for byte) fixed the review findings that landed after the merge:  the `notes_create` and `notes_update` results and `simple-notes` carry a `pin_skipped` reason when the pin was skipped, the pin lock's stale age follows `NOTES_SHORTCUT_TIMEOUT_S`, a live lock owned by another user is never taken over, and stale takeovers are capped at three per call.  Follow-up #820 (merge `705bef5b`, reinstalled at 8:44am, byte for byte) names each way the pin lock fails, so a busy lock, a lock directory that cannot be made and a lock that keeps coming back stale each get their own `pin skipped:` reason, skips the lock wait at once when the lock directory cannot be made (no more 20 s stall blamed on another run), makes the lock directory and its parent `0700`, and keeps every `pin skipped:` reason inside the 200 characters that `agent_sync.notes` keeps, so "note saved unpinned" is never cut off.  Relayed `#notes-relay` requests still do not report a skipped pin (AFC-455).  **Mon, Oct 26, 2026 (MM, AFC-552):**  the sentence gap now reaches the note.  Apple Notes normalizes a NO-BREAK space away on import, so the reference for one came back as two ordinary spaces, which the renderer collapses to one, and the gap never reached the owner.  The helper now writes the NARROW NO-BREAK SPACE, the character the fleet corpus already ruled for this surface, which the import keeps byte-intact.  The note body is HTML, so all three gap paths write the numeric reference `&#8239; ` and the note HTML carries no raw non-ASCII byte;  the mobile quick view is plain text that is embedded with html.escape, so a reference there would reach the owner as the literal six characters, and that one path writes the character itself.  The character the reference names decides whether the gap arrives, and the reference form is not the problem.  The live copy was reinstalled from `main` after the merge, byte for byte.  **The gap had been missing from every note in the Coding folder, not only from one:**  all 100 notes were read back and not one holds a no-break space in any form, no numeric reference, no named entity and no raw character, while 91 of them hold two ordinary spaces after a sentence end.  The helper reported the conversion as done while the note kept the unconverted form.  Always-on?  No: on-demand.  Always-on?  No: on-demand. |

| `~/.claude/hooks/sim-session-hook.sh` + `sim-session-hook.py` | Always-on (Claude Code hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json`:  PreToolUse, PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure with matcher `Bash\|mcp__Claude_Code_iOS_Simulator__.*`, plus Stop, SubagentStop and SessionEnd).  Enforces "never end a turn with a simulator booted" for Claude Code.  A booted-set diff around each tool call that can boot a simulator (`xcodebuild`, `simctl boot`, `open -a Simulator`, `ios-debug`, the desktop iOS Simulator MCP tools) records each new device as owned by (session, agent) in `~/Library/Application Support/sim-idle-reaper/sessions/<session>.json`;  the booted set comes from each `device.plist` state, not `simctl list`.  Stop shuts down the main agent's devices, SubagentStop a subagent's own (a parent's Stop never touches a background worker's simulator), SessionEnd everything the session owns;  shutdowns run in a detached worker, so the hook returns at once.  A session that attached the iOS Simulator panel is left to SessionEnd and the reaper;  Stop also defers while `xcodebuild`, `xctest` or `bitrig` runs or Simulator.app is open.  When Bitrig is active (app, CLI, or ACP session), exempts up to 1 shared booted simulator (most recently claimed/seen).  The first claim wins between concurrent sessions.  The `.sh` prefilter answers ordinary tool calls in about 10ms without starting Python (a matched call is about 120ms at load average 460).  Fails open, never writes stdout;  `SIM_REAPER_HOOKS=0` disables.  Log `~/Library/Logs/sim-session-hook.log`.  Tracked `scripts/hooks/`;  installed by `scripts/install-sim-idle-reaper.py` (backup `settings.json.bak-sim-reaper-<stamp>`). | `~/apps/botfleet-server-start.sh` | **Down** (verified Oct 28, 2026 — MM;  the same correction as the `app.botfleet.server` row above, which this one mirrors).  The harness this wrapper launches is held down by `~/.botfleet/stopped.marker`, written Oct 25, 2026 5:02pm CT, and nothing is listening on `:8799`/`:8800`.  The wrapper reads the marker before it starts anything and exits on the deliberate-shutdown branch, so a run finds nothing to do; with `StartInterval=300` that is a no-op every five minutes rather than a crash loop.  `~/.botfleet/startup-enabled.marker` is **absent** and `~/.botfleet/startup-disabled.marker` is present, so the launcher keeps the harness down at every tick;  the shutdown marker is the one the launcher honors first.  `~/.botfleet/current` still points at release `2f73abd1e043994d76d0f11b3d0599a60551f279` (the `/api/system/control` build from PR #1282), so promoting a newer release is still the right next step for BF-2527 once the owner wants the harness back; the previous release `724425968dcc491539cd4204011703823a4d5f3a` remains on disk for rollback.  To bring it back:  remove `~/.botfleet/stopped.marker` and `~/.botfleet/startup-disabled.marker`, then create `~/.botfleet/startup-enabled.marker`, so the next LaunchAgent tick starts it from the release. | `~/apps/update-botfleet.sh` | On-demand.  **Oct 9, 2026 (CLAUDE): resynced from `origin/main:scripts/update-botfleet.sh` at f1b5ab80f, after PR #960.**  Updates no longer wait on busy bots.  They hold new work, give running work about a minute, then pause, save and resume it, and every exit path leaves BotFleet running.  `--wait-for-idle` opts out of interrupting.  Backup: `~/apps/update-botfleet.sh.bak-20261009-pre-960`.  **Oct 8, 2026 (CLAUDE) — behaviour change in the updater it bootstraps, BotFleet branch `claude/updater-drain` (board `ef645faf`, PR #960, not merged).**  Busy bots no longer block `ubf`: apply asks the harness to hold new work, gives running work a 60-second grace (`--grace SECONDS`, `BOTFLEET_UPDATE_GRACE_MS`), then pauses and saves what is left and resumes it after the restart.  A live room conversation is never interrupted; apply waits up to 5 more minutes for it (`BOTFLEET_UPDATE_ROOM_WAIT_MS`) and then stops without updating.  `--wait-for-idle [MINUTES]` is the never-interrupt opt-in.  A non-BotFleet process holding BotFleet state is waited out for up to 90 seconds (`BOTFLEET_UNKNOWN_HOLDER_WAIT_MS`) and never signalled.  The preflight retries a slow harness for up to 60 seconds (`BOTFLEET_PREFLIGHT_RETRY_MS`).  `unquiesce` also lifts a hold.  The machine copy of this wrapper needs a resync from `origin/main` after merge: until then its up-to-date shortcut sends `--grace` and `--wait-for-idle` on to the updater instead of short-circuiting.  Machine copy untouched by this lane.  **Oct 7, 2026 9:55pm (CLAUDE) — resynced from `origin/main:scripts/update-botfleet.sh` (#842).**  The installed copy was the Oct 1 version, which does not copy `scripts/stage-entries.mjs` into the bootstrap dir, so every `ubf` died with ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND before touching anything.  Backup: `~/apps/update-botfleet.sh.bak-20261007-pre-842`.  **Oct 1, 2026 ~5pm CT (MINIMAX) — packaging is more patient about a busy Mac, and says so when it gives up.**  A `ubf`/`package:mac:local` failed on Oct 1, 2026 at a host load average of ~400-700 (swap nearly full, same saturation as board `9007ed29`) with `darwin-arm64 executable did not identify as cloudflared 2026.8.2`, even though the staged binary matched its pinned sha256 and `cloudflared version` prints in ~0.2s warm / ~1.5s cold.  `scripts/prepare-cloudflared.mjs` capped that probe at 10s and reported a missed slice as a version mismatch, so a healthy-but-saturated Mac read as a corrupt download.  The probe now waits 60s and retries once **on a timeout only** (a missing, non-executable, or wrong-version binary still fails immediately), and the thrown error carries `status=`, `signal=`, and the spawn error or a capped output excerpt, so "the host never gave it a CPU" is distinguishable from "this is not cloudflared 2026.8.2".  Same helper runs from `scripts/after-pack.mjs` `validateCloudflared`, so both call sites change.  **What this means for a seat running `ubf`:** a build on a healthy Mac is unaffected (the probe returns in well under two seconds either way); a build where the probe is genuinely wedged can now take up to ~2 minutes longer at each of the two sites before failing rather than ~10s at the first, and the retry prints one line to stderr saying it is retrying.  The Mac row is unchanged otherwise, and the download/cache behaviour in `third_party/cloudflared/README.md` is untouched.  **Synced Thu, Oct 1, 2026 5:23am CT (Claude)** from tracked `scripts/update-botfleet.sh` at `origin/main` `f6c49084` (PR #774, board `d07ad79e`, 345 lines).  Fixes `ubf` silently doing nothing: the wrapper runs the updater from `mktemp -d` under `TMPDIR` (`/var/folders/...`, and `/var` is a symlink to `/private/var`), Node realpaths the ESM entry module, and the old `import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1])` guard was false, so every `ubf`, `--force` and `--help` exited 0 with no output.  The updater now compares realpaths, and the wrapper canonicalizes the bootstrap dir with `pwd -P`.  **`--force` / `BOTFLEET_FORCE=1` warning, now documented in the wrapper and `--help`:** it is not only "reinstall even if current".  It also skips the idle requirement and force-quiesces the harness, interrupting busy bots and running or queued routines (saved to `pending-update-resume.json` and resumed after the update; a live room turn still refuses).  Backup: `update-botfleet.sh.bak-20261001-pre-realpath`.  Verified: `bash -n` passes, `~/apps/update-botfleet.sh --help` with the default `/var/folders` TMPDIR prints usage, and no update was run.  Prior: **Synced Thu, Sep 24, 2026 4:05pm CT (Claude)** from tracked `scripts/update-botfleet.sh` at `origin/main` `39528d9c` (326 lines; the host copy had been the 112-line pre-#526 wrapper without the "bootstrap the updater from the target commit" block that the bundle-ID rename rollout `docs/rollouts/2026-09-22-updater-transition-bootstrap.md` step 4 asks for).  Backup: `update-botfleet.sh.bak-20260924-pre-bootstrap`.  Verified: the always-on checkout already contains the transition commit `ce3e7c7c`, no stray `~/apps/update-botfleet-mac.mjs` exists, `bash -n` passes, no update was run.  Audit finding OP5 (`docs/audits/2026-09-24-efficiency-audit.md`).  Prior: **Replaced Sat, Sep 12, 2026 6:50pm CT (Claude)** with the repo's transactional updater: a copy of `scripts/update-botfleet.sh` from jaywedgeworth22/BotFleet at `3b30294e`, which execs the tracked `~/apps/botfleet-server/scripts/update-botfleet-mac.mjs` so the installed helper and its tests cannot drift apart.  The old non-transactional script (move checkout, `rm -rf /Applications/BotFleet.app`, `cp -a`, `kickstart`) is kept at `update-botfleet.sh.bak-20260912-safe-updater`.  No update was run as part of that install. |  Launchers are unchanged: terminal alias `ubf` (`~/.zshrc`) and the double-click wrapper `~/apps/Update BotFleet.command` both still call this path, and a bare run is still the `update` command.  What changed for the owner: an update now **prepares** (fetch, pin a commit reachable from `origin/main`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and package inside a throwaway worktree under `~/Library/Caches/BotFleet/updates/<stage>/`, then verify signature, Team ID, bundle ID, build identity, and dependency fingerprint) and only then **applies** under an authenticated quiesce — it refuses while work is in flight, fences API mutations and webhook deliveries, and sends `SIGTERM` only to BotFleet PIDs it verified first, never `SIGKILL`, `pkill`/`killall`, or the old Finder / Dock / System Settings / icon-cache cleanup.  Any install or startup failure **rolls back** the prior app bundle, dependency tree, and checkout commit.  Receipts and exactly one verified rollback generation live under `~/Library/Caches/BotFleet/updates/<audit>/`; `~/apps/update-botfleet.sh unquiesce` releases the admission fence if an interrupted run left it held.  Sub-commands: `update` (default), `prepare --target/--source/--stage [--bundle --dependencies]`, `apply --stage`, `unquiesce`; also `--no-open` and `--help`.  Carry-overs from the old script, checked at install: the Sentry DSN is no longer exported by the wrapper because the tracked updater packages through `scripts/with-sentry-dsn.sh`, which resolves `SENTRY_DSN` / `VITE_SENTRY_DSN` from the environment, then Infisical, then the same chmod 600 handoff file, disables xtrace first and never prints a value; the codesign timestamp retry is unnecessary because the new build always passes `-c.mac.timestamp=none`; and the retired `ubf --force` flag is swallowed by a small compat shim in the installed copy (the transaction has no "already at `origin/main`, skip" early exit — every run prepares and applies — and the flag would otherwise abort the run as an unknown option).  Three behaviour changes worth knowing: packaging now demands the exact `Developer ID Application: Jay Wedgeworth, LLC (CC8UTF7ATG)` identity with no Apple Development or ad-hoc fallback (present on this Mac, verified Sep 12); `apply` refuses while `~/apps/botfleet-server` shows **any** `git status --porcelain` output, untracked files included, whereas the old script first restored its own three generated paths and ignored untracked files — that checkout carried a modified `electron/vendor/electron-updater.cjs` at install time, so it must be cleaned before the first run or the update will build for minutes and then refuse; and the wrapper runs whichever implementation the live checkout holds, which is currently older than `3b30294e`, so the first run uses the pre-PR-#371 rollback placement and every run after that uses the merged one.  Rollout doc: `docs/rollouts/2026-09-12-safe-mac-updater.md` (PR #325; rollback placement and pruning fixed in PR #371 = `3b30294e`).  Tests green at that commit: `scripts/mac-update-transaction.node-test.mjs`, `scripts/update-botfleet-mac.node-test.mjs`, `scripts/mac-update-rollback-placement.node-test.mjs` (50 passing).  **Oct 1, 2026 ~11pm CT (MINIMAX) — the CUA driver probe had the same bug, one step later in the same build, and it wasted a 40MB download before failing.**  `pnpm package:mac:local` runs `package:prepare` (which stages cloudflared), then `build:speech`, `build:recorder`, then `build:cua`, so `scripts/prepare-cua.mjs` sits downstream of the probe #780 fixed and was still capping its own `cua-driver --version` check at 5s.  Worse, its only answer was `null` for every failure, so on a saturated host the cache read a timed-out probe as "this binary is wrong", deleted the cache, re-downloaded the same pinned release, probed the identical bytes again, and then threw "downloaded CUA Driver does not report version 0.20.0" — naming a download that had just been checksum-verified as corrupt.  The probe is now shared with cloudflared (`scripts/native-version-probe.mjs`, 60s + one retry on timeout only, causes classified) and **a timeout no longer re-downloads**: the cache is keyed to a pinned sha256, so the same bytes cannot change the outcome.  **What this means for a seat running `ubf`:** healthy builds are unaffected (the probe answers in well under a second, and a warm cache skips the download entirely); a genuinely wedged probe now fails fast with "the host was too busy to run them" instead of burning a release transfer first, and a build where it is merely slow can take up to ~2 minutes longer before giving up.  `prepare-cua.mjs` is now importable (it only does its work when run as the entry module), so the probe and the cache decision are unit-tested.  No LaunchAgent, pm2 job or cron row changed.

It backs up the workbook to `~/apps/fleet-ops-sheet/backups/` first and writes `~/apps/fleet-ops-sheet/last-run-registry.json` and `~/apps/FLEET-APPS-REGISTRY.md`.  Its Fleet RAG ingest half is a **partial** refresh (`recall ingest --source doc`): it takes the shared `state/ingest.lock` for its whole 90-minute budget and fails at once with exit 3 when the lock is held, which is deliberate, because the nightly has the wait and this run does not.  `FLEET_SHEET_INGEST_ENABLED=false` (the Infisical knob, read through `fleet_knobs`) is the escape hatch: it skips the ingest half and still writes the sheet.  Service account from Infisical `/google-sheets-sa` (`GOOGLE_SHEETS_SA`, a 0600 temp file for the run;  never print).  **2026-10-20 2:52am CT (MM), AFC-423: the Fleet RAG ingest step works now.**  It used to call `recall ingest <mirror path>`, and that CLI takes a source rather than a file, so the call always exited 2 and the step was a silent no-op: the corpus kept a stale copy of the registry, without the TestFlight links added under AFC-404.  It now runs `recall ingest --source doc`, which is the source that owns the mirror (the `doc` source ingests top-level `~/apps/*.md`), and the ingest is hash-skipped, so a run that changed nothing costs a walk rather than a re-embed.  Set `FLEET_SHEET_INGEST_ENABLED=false` to skip the ingest entirely. |

## Binding rule (every agent, every platform, forever)

If you **create, change, load, bootout, or retire** any of the following, you
**must** update this file **and** the Apple Note in the same change
(`~/apps/apple-notes-coding.sh --update "⭐️ Background Jobs Master List"`
— prefer `--update` on that exact title; do not mint a second note):

- `~/Library/LaunchAgents/*.plist` (and `/Library/LaunchAgents` /
  `LaunchDaemons` if you touch those)
- `crontab` / login items
- `pm2` KeepAlive jobs (`pm2 start` / `delete` / `save`)
- Any helper script under `~/apps`, `~/.claude-*`, `~/vision-worker`,
  `~/actions-runner`, or similar that other agents are expected to run

Do **not** add a process "just for this session" and leave it.  If it survives
logout or reboot, it belongs here.  If you retire one, mark it **Retired** —
do not delete the row.

Cloud / no-Mac agents: do not invent LaunchAgents.  Describe the job in the
PR and leave a Mac seat to install + list it.

Never register a daemon from `~/.npm/_npx/…`.  `mac-auto-cleanup.sh` wipes
that tree and the job crash-loops (Shellular did this 42,703× on 2026-08-13).
Install to `~/apps/<tool>-runtime` instead.

## Fleet recovery lessons (read before touching pm2)

Written after the **2026-08-21 total-degradation incident** (Grok restore, Claude
root-cause).  The Mac did **not** run out of disk (~108GB free) — it ran out of CPU
and RAM on a 16GB box, and then a poisoned pm2 dump kept the fleet from coming back.

**Memory, not disk, is what takes this box down.**  Load hit ~800 with swap at
12.4GB/14.3GB and ~400k pageouts.  `JetsamEvent-2026-08-21-021055` shows the memory
killer firing with Cursor Helper Renderer as the largest process, alongside
`shutdown_stall_2026-08-21-020046` and two dirty reboots (01:58, 02:04) with no
`.panic` file.  Under that pressure every stdio MCP server times out at 30s, the pm2
God RPC wedges, and jobs crash-loop — none of which is the individual job's fault.
Check `sysctl vm.swapusage` and `uptime` **before** debugging any single service.

**`pm2 kill` and `pm2 save` write the LIVE list over `~/.pm2/dump.pm2`.**  If the live
list is a subset, the dump is poisoned and `pm2 resurrect` can never restore the rest.
That is what happened: a DeepSeek `dsh`/`npx` session in `~/Code/Congress.Trade` started
a fresh God holding only `vision-worker` at 00:35, and the 01:05 and 01:40 kills wrote
that one-job list over the dump.  **Never `pm2 save` unless `pm2 list` shows the full 14
jobs**, and never save from a one-off `npx`/`dsh` session.  The previous good list
survives in `~/.pm2/dump.pm2.bak` — copy it aside before any pm2 surgery
(`~/.pm2/dump.pm2.golden-14apps.20260821` is the 2026-08-21 snapshot).

**`pm2 start` does NOT re-read `env` from the ecosystem file.**  pm2 caches an app's
environment on first start and replays it forever.  On 2026-08-21 Shellular crash-looped
on `/bin/sh: ioreg: command not found` — `node-machine-id` shells out to
`ioreg -rd1 -c IOPlatformExpertDevice` — because the cached PATH had no `/usr/sbin`,
even though `pm2-ecosystem.config.cjs` sets `/usr/sbin:/sbin` correctly.  Editing the
config alone changes nothing.  Use `pm2 restart <app> --update-env`, or
`pm2 delete <app> && pm2 start "$ECOSYSTEM" --only <app>`.  Verify against the LIVE env,
never the file: `pm2 jlist` → `pm2_env.env.PATH`.

**Two pm2 installs shadow each other.**  `/usr/local/bin/pm2` (7.0.1, node 24) precedes
`/opt/homebrew/bin/pm2` (7.0.3, node 26) on PATH, which yields "In-memory PM2 is
out-of-date" and a CLI that cannot drive the running God.  Prefer the explicit
`/opt/homebrew/bin/pm2`, or run `pm2 update` — but note `pm2 update` restarts every job
from the CURRENT dump, so confirm the dump is not poisoned first.

**`mac-process-watch.sh` cannot recover a poisoned dump.**  When 3+ jobs are missing it
runs `pm2 resurrect` only, and never falls through to
`pm2 start "$ECOSYSTEM" --only <name>` the way the one/two-missing path does.  Combined
with the 4-restarts/hour backoff, the watch then stops even attempting the useless
resurrect — which is why nothing self-healed overnight.  **Open follow-up:** make the
bulk path verify the expected names actually came back and start any stragglers from the
ecosystem file, and skip dump-on-kill when the live list is a subset.

**Every always-on pm2 job is in `~/apps/pm2-ecosystem.config.cjs`**, and the retired `mac-collab`, `mac-collab-litestream`,
`mac-collab-sync` and `mac-collab-writeback` (2026-10-19, AFC-363) are out of it and out of the watch list, so they must NOT come back — so
recovery never needs a hand-rolled `pm2 start`.  Restore with `pm2 start ~/apps/pm2-ecosystem.config.cjs`
and only then `pm2 save`.

**`grok-leader` vs a live Grok TUI:** if `/usr/sbin/lsof ~/.grok/leader.sock` shows a `grok-*` TUI holding the socket, **`pm2 stop grok-leader`** (not `pm2 kill`).  `mac-process-watch` skips restart while that socket is bound (`SKIP pm2:grok-leader lock-held`) and `pm2 stop`s an `errored` storm.  Bare `lsof` is a no-op when PATH lacks `/usr/sbin` (LaunchAgent 2026-08-21: 355 restarts).  `leader.sh` exits 75 when the lock is held; ecosystem `stop_exit_codes: [75]`.  Leaving the pm2 job `errored` burns CPU on a restart storm.  Start pm2 `grok-leader` again only after the TUI exits and the socket is free.  Do not `pm2 kill` the whole daemon to "fix" a lock conflict.

**`~/.pm2/pm2.log` grows unbounded** — it reached **230MB** by 2026-08-21, which makes
every read of it slow.  Read it with `grep -a … | tail`, and rotate it when it gets big.

**Agent worktrees belong in `~/apps/*`, never `~/Code/*`.**  `code-main-keeper` (pm2,
every ~60s) deliberately returns every top-level `~/Code/*` checkout to `origin/main` —
those trees are the human review / integration base, so "open in Xcode" always builds
the landed product.  On a non-main branch with a clean tree it soft-checkouts main and
fast-forwards.  It is safe by design (never hard-resets, never force-checkouts, never
discards commits absent from `origin/main`), but a worktree created at
`~/Code/<repo>-<seat>` jumps back to main the moment your tree goes clean, which reads
exactly like lost work if you have not pushed yet.  Push early, and put the worktree
under `~/apps/`.

## Not in this list (on purpose)

- Interactive `grok` / `claude` / `codex` / `cursor` terminals
- Per-session MCP children (`npm exec` MCP servers).  Those die with the parent.
  Orphans with `ppid=1` are leaks — kill those, not KeepAlive jobs, and not BotFleet
  background jobs (any process carrying `BOTFLEET_JOB_ID`): the BotFleet harness stops
  those itself (row above, "BotFleet background jobs").
- Coolify / Hetzner app containers
- iOS Simulator
- Apple system LaunchAgents

## How to add a row

1. Prefer `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.jay.<name>.plist` or a `~/apps/<tool>-runtime` + pm2 job.
2. Label, program path, KeepAlive / interval, log path, one-line purpose, Kind.
3. Never put secrets in the plist.  Inject via Infisical (`fleet-secrets`) at runtime, value-blind;  `~/.secrets` is retired.
4. Update **this file first**, then `--update` the Apple Note, then the coordinator repo mirror.
5. Do not duplicate the full table into every app's AGENTS.md — point here.

```bash
launchctl print gui/$(id -u)/<Label>
launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/<Label>
launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/<Label>
pm2 status
bash ~/apps/mac-status.sh
```

### pm2 orphan-holds-port recovery (2026-08-21, CLAUDE)

**Symptom.** One or more pm2 apps sit `errored` with a large `restart_time`, their
error log repeats `Address already in use` / `EADDRINUSE` / Deno `AddrInUse (os error 48)`,
and `pm2 jlist` / `pm2 restart` time out.

**Cause.** When the pm2 God daemon dies without reaping its children (memory pressure,
jetsam, a crash), the children are reparented to launchd and keep their listening socket.
The pm2-managed replacement can then never bind, so pm2 restart-loops it forever.  Nothing
inside that loop can clear the orphan.

**Recovery** (script: `~/apps/mac-collab/`-independent, see the CLAUDE rollout note):

1. `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.jay.mac-process-watch` -- pause the watchdog so its
   own `pm2 resurrect` cannot race the repair.  **Re-arm it afterwards with `launchctl
   bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.jay.mac-process-watch.plist`.**
2. Record God's pid (`~/.pm2/pm2.pid`) and `pgrep -P <god>` BEFORE signalling anything --
   once the parent dies the survivors reparent and you lose the list.
3. `pm2 kill`, then SIGTERM (SIGKILL after ~6s) every recorded child plus every listener on
   a fleet port.  **Fleet ports: 8765 agy-acp, 8791 xcode-health, 8792 (free since mac-collab retired 2026-10-19), 8793 seat-mcp,
   8899 senate-relay, 12419 grok-acp.**  Missing a port here is how senate-relay stayed
   broken through the first pass of this repair (238 restarts against an orphan deno on 8899).
4. `rm -f ~/.pm2/{rpc.sock,pub.sock,pm2.pid}` -- stale IPC is why a freshly started God
   still answers nothing.
5. `pm2 resurrect` (from `~/.pm2/dump.pm2`), then `pm2 jlist` to confirm 14/14 online.
6. `pm2 reset all` so the next crash-loop is visible against a zero baseline, then `pm2 save`.

**Do not** SIGKILL God first and sort it out afterwards -- that is precisely how the orphans
are created.

**pm2 `grok-leader`** is the shared Grok backend (`~/.grok/leader.sock`).  **pm2 `grok-acp`** is
`127.0.0.1:12419` for new sessions started with `acp-client.py` (`--no-leader serve` — `--leader serve`
does not bind).  Shellular Grok + new TUI join the leader.  List/load:
`python3 ~/apps/grok-acp-runtime/leader-client.py list`.
The Slack inbox (`com.jay.slack-agent-inbox`) was retired 2026-10-08; use `simple-chat inbox`.
Scout uses local `http://127.0.0.1:8899/fetch-ptr` (same session as
production).  `com.PM2` re-bootstrapped.  Claude remote-control up.
Ecosystem: `~/apps/pm2-ecosystem.config.cjs`.  Status: `bash ~/apps/mac-status.sh`.
Down/restart log: `~/Library/Logs/mac-process-watch.log`.  Watch times out `pm2 jlist` at 8s (wedged RPC used to hang the whole 120s loop), resurrects God if needed, and kills Shellular only when the cloud relay is confirmed still down on two passes at least 120s apart (see the `com.jay.mac-process-watch` row).  Also expects `grok-leader` + `mac-collab` + `mac-collab-sync` + `mac-collab-writeback` + `mac-collab-litestream`.  When 3+ jobs are missing, watch **resurrects only if the dump still lists the expected names**; a one-job leftover dump is treated as poison and watch runs `pm2 start ~/apps/pm2-ecosystem.config.cjs` then `pm2 save`.  Watch restarts
always-on jobs (pm2 resurrect / ecosystem start, launchd kickstart/bootstrap)
and keeps scheduled timers **loaded** (does not kickstart idle).  Steals
stale merge-shepherd run-locks older than 2h (the disk-janitor steal went with that job, Oct 11).  4-per-hour
backoff.  `MAC_PROCESS_WATCH_RESTART=0` is log-only.


## Server Runtime Additions (HH-165)

| Job | Cadence | Status And Contract |
|---|---|---|
| Shellular Server Host | Always-on under the systemd system unit `shellular.service`, run as the `jay` user | Re-homed from `root` to `jay` on Sun, Oct 25, 2026 under OPS-1000, at the owner's direction.  The runtime tree `<volume>/apps/shellular-runtime` is now `jay:fleet` with the setgid bit kept, so the fleet group still reads it, and `/root/apps` is an alias of the volume's `apps/` directory, so the documented `/root/apps/shellular-runtime/...` paths still resolve for root-run collectors.  The unit starts the foreground host through `bin/shellular`, which passes `--dir <volume>/Code --unknown-clients requires-approval`, with `Restart=always`, `RestartSec=15` and `TimeoutStopSec=45`, and it is enabled at boot through `multi-user.target`.  The unit is hardened the way SRV-S07 is: `UMask=0077`, `NoNewPrivileges=true`, `PrivateTmp=true`, `ProtectSystem=full`, `ProtectHome=read-only`, and `ReadWritePaths` limited to `~/.shellular`, `~/.pm2-shellular`, `<volume>/apps/shellular-runtime` and `<volume>/Code`, so the identity directory is mode 0700 and the E2EE pairing key 0600, readable only by the `jay` account the unit runs as.  One supervisor only: the pm2 daemon plus watchdog pair is retired, because the inventory records that pair as a known conflict on the Mac (MAC-P09 against MAC-L22).  `bin/status-json` now reads the unit's load state, active state and main PID instead of pm2 and keeps the collector's `name,status,pid,execpath` contract, so the Hog Hunter server supplement still resolves the Shellular row; it never emits environment, credentials or a command line.  Identity is unchanged, so the host never re-registers and the approved iPhone client keeps its pairing: the host id stays cached in `~/.shellular/config.json` and the E2EE pairing key stays beside it.  Verified live on the change: `systemctl is-active shellular` active, the relay TLS connection established, no listening port and no firewall change, and a `kill -9` of the host was restarted by systemd after the 15 s `RestartSec` delay, the new main PID appearing exactly 15 s after the kill.  **The `/root/apps` alias was missing on Oct 26, 2026 and was restored** as a symlink to the volume's `apps/` directory:  without it the Hog Hunter supplement's primary signal and the documented `/root/apps/shellular-runtime/...` collector paths do not resolve, and a server timer that reads its state through that path fails.  This does not change the Mac Shellular runtime. |
| Hog Hunter Server Snapshot Supplement | Scheduled every 15 minutes with the existing VPS watch | Source: HogHunter `scripts/server-status/`.  The wrapper holds one exclusive writer lock across the existing watch and supplemental collection.  Adds bounded coding-directory history, imports existing numeric database history and fixed tool states.  Root helper paths: `/root/vps-storage-watch-with-metrics.sh` and `/root/vps-status-supplement.py`; new directory cache under `/root/.vps-storage-watch`.  Deployed and manually verified Oct 19, 2026 at 6:01pm for HH-165.  The existing cron entry now calls that wrapper at the same cadence; the original watch and legacy database histories are preserved.  Verified 582 database observations, 13 coding/storage directory rows, and 12 tool states including running Shellular.  The new directory history needs a full day before its 24-hour deltas are available. |
| Shellular Server Watchdog | Retired; no schedule remains | The 10-minute root-crontab watchdog never actually ran: root had no crontab, so compliance inventory row SRV-C09 was stale when it read "Active".  The helper `bin/shellular-watchdog.py` is kept rather than deleted, under `<volume>/apps/shellular-runtime/var/retired-*`, and systemd's `Restart=always` now owns the restart duty, because two supervisors for one app is the pattern the inventory flags as a conflict.  Root's identity was retired in the same change: `/root/.shellular` is now `/root/.shellular.retired-20261025T222951Z` and `/root/.pm2` is now `/root/.pm2.retired-20261025T224021Z`, both recoverable. |
| Codex Operator Coordination Check | Every 15 minutes, driven by its own systemd timer | Repaired Mon, Oct 26, 2026 (MM, OPS-1000): the unit's `User=jay` change of Oct 25 broke every run, because the check script's state path is `/root/apps/agent-sync-operator-check/state` and `/root` is mode 0750, so the `jay` account cannot traverse it.  The unit runs as `User=root` again, as SRV-S07 records, with `UMask=0077`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `PrivateTmp`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=read-only` and `ReadWritePaths` unchanged, and the state directory is `root:fleet` 2750. Verified: the run completes, writes fresh state and the unit reports `Result=success`.  Owned by CODEX; MM changed the unit only to restore the documented configuration. |


## App-Owned Background Work (HH-170)

| Work | Mode | Purpose And Release State |
|---|---|---|
| Hog Hunter Directory Size Sampler | Always-on while the Mac app sampler runs; idle-only continuation plus on-demand visible refresh | **Deployed in signed v1.0.171 under HH-170 / HH-176 (release 37763b58cfea3026ec7ef530b2b52179d2ef85b9)**, running from ~/Applications/Hog Hunter.app;  the installed and verified sampler is now **v1.0.196** (tag v1.0.196, release d0804ee1a53057f7e53ee1082323771e50a74263), see the dated entry at the end of this row.  One app-owned shared directory store retains incremental cursors across menu closure and uses a serial metadata executor with bounded slices; no LaunchAgent or second scanner.  Visible foreground work uses reduced slices under warning pressure, while idle continuation requires normal pressure, modest load and swap below 90%; critical pressure pauses I/O.  Complete totals and original walk times persist through the coalesced history writer.  Debug builds use a separate identity, automated harnesses suppress default live probes and normal process metadata avoids automatic external icon reads.  Exact-head hosted tests, review and official release signature checks passed.  The installed v1.0.171 bundle was re-verified on Oct 20, 2026 at 7:06pm as byte-identical to the published signed and notarized release asset, with exactly one running process and no duplicate installed copies.  Full real External aggregate and sustained daily cadence remain unverified under HH-170.  Hidden-view continuation has a 15-second minimum interval; complete rows refresh on a 15-minute start-to-start cadence.  Visibility follows whether the view is open, not whether another app covers the window.  **Wed, Oct 21, 2026 (MM, HH-182):** the installed sampler is the official signed and notarized v1.0.174, tag v1.0.174 at be1aa13883c88a35d628475809068b52bc65e8a2.  The installed bundle was re-verified against the published asset and is byte for byte identical, with exactly one running process, Developer ID Application team CC8UTF7ATG, a stapled notarization ticket and Gatekeeper accepted as Notarized Developer ID;  the live appcast serves 1.0.174 and Sparkle auto-update stays on because the bundle came from a tag cut from main.  The replaced v1.0.171 bundle is in the system trash and stays recoverable.  A bounded 30 second parent-process sample taken after the install averaged 0.088% of ten-core machine CPU with a 36.1 MB footprint and zero reads and zero writes;  that is one quiet window and not a long term maximum.  The same release also shipped the dashboard navigation and menu bar window rework under HH-182.  **Tue, Oct 27, 2026 (MM, HH-226):**  the installed sampler is the official signed and notarized **v1.0.196**, tag v1.0.196 at d0804ee1a53057f7e53ee1082323771e50a74263, installed at about 6:39am CT;  the bundle is Developer ID Application team CC8UTF7ATG, `spctl` reports Notarized Developer ID, with exactly one running process and no duplicate installed copies.  It replaced v1.0.178. |
| Hog Hunter Automatic Disk Clean Regimen | Opt-in and off by default; runs inside the Mac app while it is open, on a 15-minute check | **Deployed in signed v1.0.178 under HH-183 (release 3165a3f048eb71c39309f6e81aa3570aa94080dc, tag v1.0.178)**, running from ~/Applications/Hog Hunter.app;  the installed and verified build is **v1.0.196** (tag v1.0.196, release d0804ee1a53057f7e53ee1082323771e50a74263).  App-owned, no LaunchAgent and no second scanner;  the runner is started and stopped by the store, so it lives with the app rather than with a window, and it drives a task loop instead of a run loop timer.  Enabling it in the Disk Cleaner window schedules the first run one interval out;  it never fires an immediate unattended clean, and **Run Now** is the explicit path.  A scheduled run waits for 30 idle minutes and for a user to be present;  Run Now skips both.  One APFS snapshot is taken per run rather than per chunk, and the run stops when it cannot take one.  A run that steps aside for a build or for the shared housekeeper lock at ~/.claude-disk-janitor/.housekeeper.lock did not happen, so it does not record a last run time and the next 15-minute check retries instead of waiting a whole interval.  A failed safety gate is not a completed run either:  it is remembered under its own defaults key `hoghunter.cleaner.regimen.lastGateFailureAt`, never the completed run clock, and because the gate is checked after the disk scan a persistent failure backs off for an hour instead of paying a full scan every 15 minutes.  The published next run is the later of that backoff and the interval next run, so the panel never publishes a time that has already passed.  The panel keeps every fact:  a cancelled run still says what could not be removed before the stop and never claims an item was refused, a run that removed something and then skipped says both halves, and the raw error text stays in a field no view shows because a safety check can name the absolute path it refused to delete.  The Mac re-applies the current exclusions and its own safety checks before it deletes, and thinning APFS local snapshots stays Mac-only.  **Wed, Oct 21, 2026 (MM, HH-183):** the installed bundle is the official signed and notarized v1.0.178, tag v1.0.178 at 3165a3f048eb71c39309f6e81aa3570aa94080dc.  The installed bundle was re-verified against the published asset and is byte for byte identical, with exactly one running process, Developer ID Application team CC8UTF7ATG, a stapled notarization ticket and Gatekeeper accepted as Notarized Developer ID;  the live appcast serves 1.0.178 and Sparkle auto-update stays on because the bundle came from a tag cut from main.  The replaced v1.0.174 bundle is in the system trash and stays recoverable.  A bounded 30 second parent-process sample taken after the install averaged 2.52% of one core and 0.25% of ten-core machine CPU with a footprint between 19 and 33 MB;  that is one quiet window and not a long term maximum.  The full macOS suite passes at 825 tests with 0 failures.  **Tue, Oct 27, 2026 (MM, HH-226):**  the installed bundle is the official signed and notarized **v1.0.196**, tag v1.0.196 at d0804ee1a53057f7e53ee1082323771e50a74263;  the bundle is Developer ID Application team CC8UTF7ATG, `spctl` reports Notarized Developer ID, with exactly one running process and no duplicate installed copies.  It replaced v1.0.178. |
